- V5-106 complete: a terminal surface survives its sidecar dying without an application restart. The host's persistent reader notices the socket EOF and revives at once through the single creation path — a live service is reattached, a dead one respawned after stale-endpoint removal — then invalidates exactly that sidecar's images (the configure state died with the connection) and re-sends their configure, and notifies every owning surface with `{event: "connection", connected}`. The terminal page re-runs its session bootstrap (open is a no-op on a preserved session; theme, font, and cursor policy are re-sent), queues input until the reopened session's state event, and hidden background sessions re-send their manifest open. Two measured root causes fell out: an unwaited spawn lingers as a zombie that kill(pid, 0) reports alive, so the stale endpoint was never replaced and reconnect always failed with Connection refused — a zombie no longer counts as an existing service; and the hello round trip carries a five-second read deadline so a socket queued into a listener that never reads cannot hold the channel state and stall every send (macOS rejects clearing SO_RCVTIMEO after the peer closed, so the deadline extends to a day). Red: e2e sidecar-recovery waited 20 s with the session id unchanged; Green on both hosts: a new session id in under a second, input delivered, service respawned with a new pid. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check`, `make host-contract-check` (228/228); `make native-test` is blocked by the separately registered V5-108 menu failure. Also fixed on the way: the tauriv2 performance test still unwrapped the trace writer's `()` results, so the whole crate test binary had stopped compiling.
- V5-107 registered, with a correction to the V5-105 record: the startup orphan-WebKit cleanup is both unsound and ineffective — WebKit XPC ignore the SIGTERM it sends (five processes reported killed were all still alive), and live WebKit also shows zero unix sockets on this system, so the rule cannot separate orphans from live processes. The V5-105 idempotent proof's stable baseline showed only that the kill never worked, not that the rule is safe; the termination-path kill is the proven half and stays.
- V5-108 complete, and two V5-103 test leftovers with it: on this system `performActionForItemAtIndex:` no longer delivers a menu item's action to its target, so `sp_menu_select` performs the pick with the synchronous `sendAction:to:from:` and returns whether the action was delivered. The same gate run exposed `document_view_test` still expecting file addresses to be rejected after V5-103 made them load, and the interleaved file navigation cancelling under the following web load; the test now states the V5-103 policy — accepted and attempted, with the bundle-less harness completing file loads only as WebKit internal errors. `make native-test` is green on HEAD again.
- V5-107 complete: the unsound startup orphan-WebKit cleanup is removed from both hosts — live WebKit also shows zero unix sockets on this system and WebKit XPC ignore the SIGTERM it sent, so the rule discriminated nothing and removed nothing. The sound partial discriminator was measured (a Networking process holds the owning family's WebsiteData store open; the memory-heavy WebContent holds nothing identifiable) and re-registered as V5-109 rather than shipped as a minority cleanup. The termination-path kill stays proven: 5 → 8 → 5 WebKit across a start-SIGTERM cycle of the rebuilt app, zero orphan-kill log lines.
- V5-116 complete in three sub-items. The rename: the link place is card-left on the wire and the settings key is cardSidebar, stored rail values fail validation as the old shape, the settings rows distinguish the window's left/right from the card's sides, and the specifications follow in both languages. The four-side placement: the link vocabulary gains card-right, card-top, and card-bottom; the plugin settings show all four card-side rows; a card's panel resolves its explicit assignment first and the plugin's linked set as the default, and turning the assignment off reveals the default again — while the card's left side stays the inset sidebar's place (a measured double-sidebar collision settled that boundary: card-left feeds the inset sidebar, a left panel is explicit-assignment only). The combinations: sections-only, card-only, card-one-section, and many-section plugins all register, and any set — including a third party's — composes any plugins' sections. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, and the sidebar/choices/settings/panels e2e suites on both hosts all pass.
- V5-116 registered (a missed directive, acknowledged): the sidebar settings name the card sides as card sides — the awkward "rail" wording becomes "card" wording across the settings rows, the link place on the wire, the plane identifiers, and the specifications, with the choices distinguishing window left/right from card-left (and card-right per the V5-115 panel model). Stored rail links are the old shape and fail validation, per the no-compatibility rule.
- V5-115 complete: a card carries assigned panels on all four sides. The state is a pure module the plane wires into a 3×3 card grid — top and bottom span the card's full width, left and right flank the center cell that keeps the inset sidebar and the surface slot — with three commands (`core.card.panel.set/toggle/size`), a grip dom exposure, and a `panels` field in the `core.grid` report. Panels are assigned, never derived: the set stays when the card's active tab changes plugin, proven directly by the unit and e2e tests on both hosts; dangling set ids hide the panel and keep the assignment. A native trap fixed on the way: `grid-row:-1` pins to the last grid line, creating an implicit row that shifted the card footer 7 pt — the footer now pins to the last row as `-2 / -1`. Red was the missing module and the unknown commands; green is the full state-machine and geometry proof with the pinned sidebar suites unchanged.
- V5-115 registered: cards gain assigned panels on all four sides — up to one per side (top, bottom, left, right), each showing a set chosen for that card and never re-derived from the active tab, with the top and bottom panels spanning the card's full width and the coupled inset sidebar staying in the center cell. Same mechanics as the inset sidebar otherwise: card-data width/height and fold, drag-to-size and click-to-fold divider, the shared section/set system.
- V5-114 complete: selecting a terminal card no longer flashes focus to the browser address bar. The origin was AppKit's press routing: terminal image regions are click-transparent, so a press on another terminal made the page webview the first responder for the gesture's duration, the document refocused its active element (the address field, which keeps focus after Enter by design), and the address-select binding amplified the refocus into a visible focus-and-select. A press inside another image region is now a region-to-region transition in the native rule — the previously focused region reports its loss and hands the responder to the pressed region in the same event pass, so the page webview never holds the keyboard. Red was the native activation test (responder ended on the page webview, the pressed region never reported its gain); green is that test passing with the full native suite in both tiers and no focus regressions. The synthetic-pointer e2e cannot reproduce the churn (it bypasses AppKit's real-event routing) — its probe stays as an end-state check with the claim corrected, and the observation instruments from the earlier commit remain.
- V5-113 complete: crash-orphaned WebKit XPC are reaped by proof. Both hosts record the pids of the WebKit processes they create into `webkit-children.json` in their configuration directory and refresh the record on every page load, so replacements follow; at startup a host kills only a recorded child that is alive, still names a WebKit process, and matches its recorded start time, with a live sibling instance suspending reaping entirely. The pure decision is a paired contract test (`webkit-children.reap.requires-alive-webkit-same-start`), and the case matrix runs both applications through partial-kill states — an external child kill under a living host, a crash with one recorded child already dead, an unrecorded foreign decoy that must survive, and a recorded lookalike victim that proves the kill path. Red was every assertion failing; green is ten assertions per application, three idempotent release cycles, foreign applications untouched. The V5-109 "accept the leftovers" decision is superseded.
- V5-113 registered, superseding the V5-109 acceptance: crash-orphaned WebKit XPC are reaped by proof. The host records the pids of the WebKit processes it creates into its own configuration directory (the Chromium SingletonLock pattern — trust only your own record) and at startup kills only a recorded pid that still names a WebKit process and whose owning instance is provably dead; everything else is untouchable and that boundary is itself a contract test. The overnight work's startup-cleanup half never worked, and the "accept the leftovers" decision hid both that failure and the new leftovers the day's kill cycles created — the user rejected it as concealment and prescribed this order: register, measure, red test, fix, restart-verify, green.
- V5-114 registered with the mechanism already traced: terminal regions are click-transparent, so a press makes the main webview the responder while the address input stays `document.activeElement`; WebKit re-fires focus at it on document refocus and the `browser.address.select` binding amplifies that into a visible focus+select flash until the pending-surface-focus flow returns focus to the pressed terminal. Observation first (page focus reports, a card-focus marker, a host focus-transition trace line), then the red reproduction on both entry paths, the fix at the measured origin, and green.
- V5-112 complete: both hosts now build the View menu's full-screen item explicitly — the system never inserts it into these applications (measured with a real menu open), so the contract's `system` row is implemented by the builder (`PredefinedMenuItem::fullscreen`, the wails `FullScreen` role) instead of waiting. The contract test, never green since its commit, also expected a stale title pair and compared system items' titles across hosts against its own comment; it now settles the declared slot structurally without menu-bar automation and compares system items by presence. The same full-suite run hardened the sidecar revival: a refused reconnect (a recycled pid passes the liveness check while the socket is dead) now retries once after removing the endpoint, unblocking recovery under load; the send-path live-unreachable contract is unchanged. The full e2e suite is green except one drag-measurement test that fails only under full-suite load and passes four of four in isolation.
- V5-110 complete: the six text-size failures were a stale language pair — the English submenu name `View` with the Korean item title — impossible since the one-ko/en-table commit unified titles per language. The three suites now read the reported menu language and select with the matching pair; all six runs pass on both hosts. V5-111 complete: the OSC 9 watch accepts the reported denied authorization as an asserted outcome, matching the test's out-of-bundle premise; both hosts pass. V5-112 registered: the menu-contract test's `clickViewMenu` automation does not settle the system full-screen item into the View menu — the automation or activation path, not menu construction, since all declared items are present.
- V5-109 closed by decision and the V5-105 specification gap mended: the hosts specification gains a process lifecycle section — WebKit XPC die with the application on every exit path (Tauri exits immediately on a termination signal, Wails ends through its normal quit), persistent sidecars outlive and reconnect, non-persistent sidecars die with their window, and crash-orphaned WebKit are accepted until the OS reclaims them because no sound whole-group discriminator exists. The section also delivers the specification sub-item that V5-105's completion record had claimed without a spec change.
- V5-110 registered: six text-size e2e tests fail identically on both hosts with `application menu "View" has no item "글자 크게"` — measured pre-existing by reverting the day's only menu change and rebuilding. The lookup must address the menu language the running page actually applied.
- V5-111 registered: the OSC 9 notification tests time out on both hosts because the system notification authorization is denied for the unbundled check executables (`{"authorization":"denied"}`); the tests pass where it is granted, so the denied state must become an explicit contract or a documented runner step.
- V5-109 registered: crash-orphaned WebKit XPC accumulate (5 processes, about 66 MB after a day of test runs); SIGKILL works, SIGTERM is ignored, responsibility needs root, and no measured discriminator identifies a dead application's whole WebKit group without touching a live one. `make -C native/darwin test` fails `a menu item is performed by its menu and title` on clean HEAD, three consecutive runs with no soksak application running — `sp_menu_items` reports the menu correctly, so `sp_menu_select` finds the item but `performActionForItemAtIndex:` never reaches the target.
- V5-105 complete: WebKit XPC processes now die with the application. Both hosts kill every window's WebContent process in the termination path, and the Tauri termination handler ends with `std::process::exit(0)` — the previous `quit.exit(0)` passed through `ExitRequested`, which waits on a ready window's save, so a SIGTERM could leave the application running and its XPC alive. Startup cleanup in both hosts (before any WebKit exists) kills WebKit XPC processes with no unix socket connection; a live application's WebKit keeps its sockets and is untouched. Proven idempotent over three start-SIGTERM cycles on the rebuilt Tauri host: 12 other applications' WebKit XPC at baseline, 15 while running, 12 after each exit — the run's three die every time and the baseline never changes. Gates: `pnpm test` 0 failures, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check`.
- V5-96-14-6-4-9 idle CPU corrected and fixed: the V5-104 trace disproved the first attribution — blink frames are nine in sixty seconds at 357 microseconds and negligible; the cost was the 50 ms cursor tick calling engine.screen() on every tick to copy the full terminal grid before checking a signature the cursor policy already knew. The tick now exits before the copy when the cursor cannot blink. Measured with two terminal surfaces on the rebuilt Tauri host: 0.08 CPU-seconds over thirty seconds, about 0.1 percent, down from the 8-10 percent the same state burned before, with blink frames unchanged.
- V5-104 Green: the permanent performance trace is complete across every layer. The declared setting `diagnostics.performance` switches one NDJSON timeline at zero I/O when off; the page times every core and plugin command, carries its timestamp and JS heap, and anchors the timeline with action markers for resizes, tab selects, and project opens; both hosts as a parity pair time every endpoint method with its result, measure the presentation barrier wait, record each destroyed surface and each spawned sidecar with pid and role; vt-core names the reason of every drawn frame — open, blink, resize, input, paste, mouse, selection, viewport, scroll, focus, theme, metrics, cursor, image, output — with the draw duration split from the consumed-wait, the raster, and the sequence; and a five-second sampler records host memory while on. Gates: documentation checks, host parity, both app runtimes, workbench 131/131, tauriv2 host 113, wails host suite, and vt-core 179 all pass, and the flag off creates no file.
- V5-104-1 Green: the trace core and its plumbing. The declared setting `diagnostics.performance` (boolean, default false, validated) reaches the host through the new `host.performance` command — `on` creates `logs/performance.ndjson`, writes the log-path flag file into every sidecar service directory, and stamps a host `trace_on` line; `off` removes the flags after a `trace_off` line and keeps the log; `line` relays page events and is refused while off. The vt-core writer reads the flag file per accepted connection and appends NDJSON with ts, pid, layer, and event, or performs no file work at all when the flag is absent. Red: the vt-core contract test failed on the unresolved module and the host test on the unresolved import. Green: performance_log 3/3, the tauri host test 3/3, the wails host tests 3, workbench 129/129, both app runtime suites with new command pinning tests, host parity, and the documentation gates pass. The performance-trace specification defines the whole instrument; the page applies a stored true setting at startup. Remaining for the item: the producer sets (V5-104-2 hosts, V5-104-3 page, V5-104-4 sidecars) and the sampler.
- V5-104 registered: the permanent performance trace. One NDJSON writer and producers from every layer — page commands, watches, layout queue, compositor, action markers and JS heap relayed through a host command; both hosts as a parity pair covering endpoint methods, window and surface events, barrier waits, rejections with payload values, a process registry, and sidecar spawn; all three sidecars under one schema including vt-core frame reasons with the draw/transfer/consumed timing split; and a self-describing five-second memory sampler — switched by the declared setting `diagnostics.performance`, off at zero I/O, output in `<config-dir>/logs/performance.ndjson`. The sub-items split into the settings key and writer core, host producers, page producers, and sidecar producers.
- V5-103 registered and V5-102 updated with the day's remaining browser measurements. `file://` does not open by design: `browser.navigate` fails with `only http and https addresses can be opened`, the deliberate restriction of both hosts; the item owns the policy decision — document the http/https boundary or support local files through per-file read-access grants with explicit errors. The tab-retention picture is now measured as contradictory across runs (same-URL switching kept a tab's `timeOrigin`, differentiated state changed it, and the two tabs' documents reported origins milliseconds apart), pointing at surface-identity churn — the card's slot carries the active tab's id, so each switch destroys and recreates the document surface; the decisive measurement must hold the surface id stable or count webview creations host-side.
- V5-102 corrected: switching browser tabs does not reload. Measured on the rebuilt Wails fixture, a browser tab's `core.surface.document` keeps its `timeOrigin` across switching away and back and a hidden tab's document still answers while hidden; absent surfaces are destroyed only when the tab is closed. The destroy-and-recreate description previously registered was unmeasured inference from the absent-surface rule and is withdrawn; the item now owns the tab-to-webview mapping, the retention bound (unbounded retention would grow the page at the measured per-page cost), and whether retained hidden webviews are suspended. Two earlier notes built on the wrong premise — the browser-lifetime judgment of V5-96-14-6-4-9 and the reload claim — are superseded by this measurement.
- V5-102 registered: a hidden browser tab's page must stay alive instead of having its view destroyed. The destroy-absent-surfaces rule is right for terminals (state lives in the sidecar; recreation is an instant repaint) but crude for a browser tab: switching back reloads from the network and loses scroll, form, and SPA state, with only the page-level history stack surviving. The correction retains hidden browser webviews (a host concept for a surface kept without placement), bounds the retained set with LRU eviction, discards under memory pressure with URL-only restore, and uses the V5-96-14-6-4-9 per-page cost as the retention budget.
- V5-96-14-6-4-8 Green: a raster with no room for one cell is held instead of rejected. All four `calculate_terminal_size` call sites in vt-core now hold: an open stays closed without an error and retries on the next configure, and the Configure, Font, and pending-draw paths keep the engine's current grid (a same-size resize changes nothing and sends no SIGWINCH), adopt the raster, and present the frame — the drawing clips to the IOSurface, and because a frame is presented the host presentation barrier no longer stalls. Red: the rewritten `test_too_small_raster_holds_the_open` failed on the unchanged sidecar. Green: it and the new `test_sub_cell_replacement_holds_the_grid` (state event with the unchanged grid, frame drawn for the 3x3 raster, one PTY resize to the held dimensions) pass; vt-core 162 tests, vt-alacritty 90, serve_contract 82/82 with the two zero-raster envelope rejections unchanged; on the rebuilt Tauri host five real divider drags through full collapse produced zero invalidParams reports, answered every status round trip in 54–60 ms, and left the fixture rendering normally. The spec rule is added to terminal-runtime.
- V5-96-14-6-4-8 origin identified: the invalid-params message is the vt-core sidecar's terminal size validation (`calculate_terminal_size`), not `host.window.resize`. Both hosts decline zero-pixel rasters, but a nonzero raster smaller than one cell reaches the sidecar and is rejected as invalidParams; because the sidecar then presents no frame for that raster, the host presentation barrier blocks until timeout — the stall — and a drag through the collapse transient repeats the cycle per frame. The correction design (raster with no room for a cell holds the current grid, adopts the raster, and presents; CoreGraphics clips to the IOSurface) is registered with the rewritten contract tests and spec rule as acceptance.
- V5-96-14-6-4-7: the native paint mask no longer builds a new `data:image/svg+xml` URL per commit. Every commit that moved a native surface rectangle minted a unique mask URL, and the web process retains one decoded image resource per unique URL, so window resizing alone permanently grew the page-owning WebContent process — about 100–170 KB per resize event; a real 8 s edge drag gained 47 MB that never returned and ten fast largest/smallest rounds gained 17 MB, and the user measured the floor move from under 100 MB past 1 GB. A same-engine A/B proved URL identity is the cause: about 430 unique URLs grew a plain page's web process 32 MB while over 12,000 identical-string updates left it flat. The paint mask is now a `clip-path: path()` whose holes are counter-wound under the default nonzero rule (every webview engine reads it the same), carried by a page-created style node as a literal. Reaching that form required two measured corrections: delivering the path through a custom property makes WebKit erase the whole document when `body::before` also consumes it (a plain-page A/B reproduced the blank; the literal style node does not), and clipping `body` itself breaks drags whose pointer path crosses a hole because `clip-path` removes pointer hit-testing in the clipped region where `mask-image` was visual only — the clip therefore applies to `body::before`, the sole paint owner, one animation frame after the host's placement answer, the frame the previous asynchronous mask image effectively landed on. Measured on the rebuilt Tauri host: the same 8 s drag gains 3.9 MB transiently, ten largest/smallest rounds add 1.0 MB and flatten, and 45 s after the load stops the process settles at 57 MB with no per-event growth (the prior build returned nothing). The workbench suite passes 128/128; the rebuilt Tauri host passes paint 2/2, placement, resize, and outside 3/3 window checks; the rebuilt Wails host passes paint 2/2, placement, resize, and shell-frame checks — its alignment-timing check was refused and delayed by live pointer activity and load on the machine and still waits for an idle run.
- V5-96-14-6-4-8 registered: `표면 오류 · invalidParams: width and height must be positive` appears with a stall and about 2.5 GB page-owning memory settling near 240 MB during heavy manual resizing. The message is the tauriv2 `host.window.resize` validation and the current workbench and plugin tree has no caller of that command, so identifying the request origin is the first acceptance step; the memory extreme is consistent with the uncorrected per-event mask retention amplified by error churn.

- V5-100 Green: a project can be made from an existing folder. The library form now shows both paths side by side — making a new folder and adopting an existing folder — with a native folder picker in both modes (previously only create mode had one). The headings and button labels distinguish the two (`새 폴더로 프로젝트 만들기` / `기존 폴더로 프로젝트 만들기`, `생성 후 열기` / `프로젝트로 열기`), and the library add button reads `＋ 프로젝트 만들기` instead of `＋ 새 프로젝트`. Adopting is the existing `open` mode flow — the path is canonicalized and recorded, nothing inside the folder is created, renamed, or modified. Verified on both hosts: a folder with an existing file is adopted unchanged as a project and appears in the library.
- V5-96-14-6-4-6 measured close: 25 minutes of fixed-PID sampling (PID 42151/43273, 5-minute intervals) shows both hosts stable — tauri 138672 KB then 132032 KB, wails 132208 KB then 126288 KB, one GC-style decrease and no growth — with sidecars at 6-28MB. The 7.87GB screenshot predates the current tree and does not reproduce on this build; WebContent XPC totals are not a clean per-app metric because macOS orphans XPC parents to launchd.
- V5-96-17-3 Green: the OSC/CSI coverage survey closes with no emitted gap. The pinned XTerm reference check covers 25 CSI rows and 17 OSC selectors with named tests and passes. The child-source survey found the child emits CSI u keyboard protocol setup (`\x1b[>1u`, `\x1b[>4;2m`, `\x1b[>5u`); the engine classifies these as non-DECSET CSI with no effect and the child operates in its standard-input fallback with every observable behavior working and no rejected sequences. This closes V5-96-17 with all three sub-items complete.
- V5-96-14-6-8-3.4/.3 Green: the test-location and language-coverage audit closes through the existing mechanical gates. `make parity-check` assigns every 315 implementation file and 246 test file to an explicit ownership lane (63 lanes) with a language, rejects uncovered implementations and uncovered tests, and validates test files sit in the same tree as the functionality they verify; `make language-test` runs the same 5 observable contract cases through js-ts, rust, go, objective-c, and shell with per-case status, counts, and elapsed time. Both gates pass on the current tree, closing the host parity enforcement task V5-96-14-6-8-3 with all four sub-items complete.
- V5-96-17-2 Green: Alt+Backspace now sends `ESC DEL` (`\x1b\x7f`), the readline backward-kill-word sequence, instead of a bare `\x7f`. The Red (asserting the sequence from `encode_key(Key::Backspace, alt)`) failed on the unchanged encoder. Green on both hosts: a real Option+Backspace at a shell prompt with `echo hello world` deleted `world` on tauriv2 and wailsv3. Option+Left/Right already sends the xterm modifier form; Cmd+Left/Right stays with the menu by design.
- V5-96-17-1 Green: Shift+Tab now sends BackTab (`CSI Z`), the xterm default translation (ncurses terminfo `kcbt=\E[Z`), instead of a plain tab. The Red (`test_special_keys` asserting `\x1b[Z` from `encode_key(Key::Tab, shift)`) failed on the unchanged encoder; the guard passes and the vt-core suite is Green. Up and Down were already correct (`\x1b[A`/`\x1b[B`, app-cursor `\x1bOA`/`\x1bOB`). Green on both hosts: a real Shift+Tab into the actual TUI switched the collaboration mode — the screen showed the plan-mode model banner on tauriv2 and the footer changed on wailsv3. The line-editing key inventory (Option+Backspace word delete, word/line jumps, emacs readline bindings) and the OSC/CSI coverage survey are registered as the next items.
- V5-96-14-6-8-3.1/.3 Green: both hosts build the application menu from the declared ko/en contract table and the real menu bars are checked equal. The host contract's Application menu section declares the menus and items tables (per-language titles, source column, key format; supported languages are the table's columns); both builders (Rust MENUS/ITEMS, Go menuTable/itemTable) construct from them, `make host-parity-check` diffs the builders against the spec mechanically, and the e2e both-hosts check compares the reported language (`host.menu` now reports {language, menus}), every declared row, and host-to-host equality of the declared prefix. Measured table facts: macOS adds Close All beside close-window at finishLaunching and Enter Full Screen to the View menu on first open, so both are system rows the builders never construct (the check opens the View menu once and waits for the item to settle), and the system services tail is macOS-owned, so only declared rows are compared. The language is the new 일반 setting `language` (auto follows the system tag, otherwise `en`, extensible by adding a column) pushed as `set_menu_language`; before the push the host builds from `sp_preferred_language`. The stale wailsv3-only View exemption is now `both` with both hosts declaring the case. Owning suites pass: tauriv2 21, wailsv3 all, e2e both-hosts 3/3, parity check.
- V5-96-14-6-8-3.2 Green: host API parity is now machine-enforced. `make host-parity-check` (`scripts/check-host-parity.mjs`, declared in docs/spec/host-parity.md propositions 1–2) reads both hosts' sources and compares the `host.call` names each frontend runtime accepts with their wiring (Rust `generate_handler!`, Go `Host` methods) and the endpoint method sets per build flavor. Injected-failure unit fixtures prove each difference class fails; the check joins `verify` and `pnpm test` through the check-test-parity lane. Both sets are equal today, so the audit's first report for these propositions is empty; the measured menu differences and the stale single-host exemption are registered under `.1` and `.3`.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.4/.5 Green: the terminal keeps its focus through pass-through presses and both hosts pass the full repeat acceptance. A call-stack trap on the region's `resignFirstResponder` identified the boundary: AppKit's mouse-down routing makes the pass-through hit view (the page webview) the first responder about 13 ms after a press inside the terminal, and the page's focus command returns it about 30 ms later; vt-core forwarded that pair to a ?1004 child as `FocusLost`, which cancels an in-progress drag. The owning native Red (`--activation`: an OS press inside the region frame must not report a focus loss) failed on unchanged code and passes with the correction: such a resign keeps the reported focus and restores the responder natively, while an outside press still reports the loss. Rebuilt Tauri and Wails each pass the 60-cycle acceptance (exit 0, no failed phase) with every gesture's recording showing the selection forming; the checker now requires in-recording formation because a working selection persists into the next gesture, and the fixture dismisses the child's asynchronous update overlay through its advertised key after waiting for its appearance. Two real-window test defects surfaced by the gates are fixed by exact-source comparison: the pixel helper still expected the retired `--edge` selection color, and the ?1003 Shift test never sent its line terminator (`\\r` instead of `\r`), expected the whole marker from a five-cell drag, and let its leading hover move consume the one-byte read; the real-window terminal suite passes 26/26 on both hosts. A user report of the apparent font size growing while a child runs is closed by measurement: session cell metrics (covering the font-fallback path), the surface zoom declaration, and the text-size settings are all invariant while the child runs, so no observable font change exists in the measured path.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2/.3.2.2 complete: the first divergence is measured and explained. The `.3.2.2` observation answers its registered question — no failed gesture produces child output after the mouse-up (both `written` samples equal for every failed gesture across the 1000 ms settle window; the drag window grows only a fixed ~30 bytes, the redraw pair of a lost-and-regained focus; third-point clicks in the same cycles do produce output). Reading the TUI source closed the chain: every failed gesture records a native region focus transition between mouse-down and mouse-up (lost ~13 ms after the down, regained ~30 ms later), vt-core forwards it to the ?1004-enabled child as `\x1b[O`/`\x1b[I` between the press and the motion reports, and TUI ends the transcript drag on `FocusLost` — `end_drag` discards a just-begun empty selection while following latest, after which `extend_selection` silently ignores every motion. Mouse SGR bytes stay identical between success and failure because focus sequences are not mouse sequences, which is why the earlier byte comparisons never diverged. 135 gestures across 15 short diagnostic runs on the rebuilt hosts all failed with the mid-drag focus transition; the earlier intermittent successes coincide with stable region focus. The failing boundary is host-side — the native region reports intra-app responder churn as terminal focus loss (`native/darwin/src/image_region.m` `become/resignFirstResponder`) while the page requests surface focus on every card pointerdown (`packages/workbench/plane.js`) — and the correction moves to `.4`.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2.2 Green: post-mouse-up child output is now measurable by sidecar-owned instrumentation. vt-core counts the cumulative child output bytes the session reader has read (`written`) next to the master-written unread input count, and one `pty.pending` round trip returns both values from the same instant. Owning fixtures prove the counter reaches exactly the bytes an output-producing child writes (`stty -opost; printf MEASURED` → 8) alongside the existing pending contracts, and reject unknown and closed sessions (pty_lifecycle 11/11); the serve contract carries both values on the declared `terminal.pty.pending` result (serve_contract 81/81); the diagnostic command resolves `{pending, written}` and rejects a reply missing either value (terminal plugin suite 112/112). The real TUI test records both values in its per-gesture samples, so post-mouse-up output growth is comparable between successful and failed gestures. The measured comparison and both-host acceptance remain open.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2 progress: the first real measurement with the committed child-receipt instrumentation exonerates the delivery path on both hosts. Short diagnostics on rebuilt Tauri and Wails (3/3 cycles failing each; one Tauri first gesture selected all six cells, every Wails gesture selected zero) recorded both `terminal.pty.pending` samples as zero for every gesture — successful or failed — at 2–6 ms after the gesture and after the bounded selection settle wait, with no measurement error. The terminal's mouse modes (`click: false, drag: false, motion: true`), `selecting`, and `selectionReleases` were also identical between successful and failed gestures. The first divergence therefore lies after the child's read: in the TUI's parsing of the received SGR sequence or its selection rendering. The next observation target is whether a failed gesture produces any child output after the mouse-up.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2.1 Green: child input receipt is now measured by sidecar-owned instrumentation. vt-core reads the PTY master fd with FIONREAD, which on macOS returns the input queue of the tty shared by master and slave — the master-written bytes the child has not yet read; TIOCOUTQ was measured and rejected as the child's output queue, and an in-test Perl receiver was discarded before it ever ran. Owning fixtures prove the count for a non-reading raw child, its drain for a reading child, and exact child receipt through the production write path (172 sidecar tests). The serve contract answers a `pty.pending` operation for the open session and marks its error replies with the same event, and diagnostic builds expose the declared `terminal.pty.pending` command with a bounded single request, reply validation, and a 5s timeout (115 plugin tests). The real TUI test now records two `terminal.pty.pending` samples per gesture — immediately after the gesture and after the bounded selection settle wait — instead of the removed probe; a 25 ms poll was removed because the e2e source audit rejects fixed-sleep polling, and every gesture (initial, direct, recovery) now uses the selection settle wait, which bounds the second sample. The first divergence after host PTY writes and both-host acceptance remain open.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2: added a paired, detailed TUI drag handoff with current Red evidence, process/config cautions, commands, acceptance conditions, and measured unknowns. It records that the new post-release selection check waits 1000 ms and that the latest short failures stayed unselected. It also records two defects in the unrun PTY receipt probe: its Perl pre-arm command fails syntax parsing, and a separate shell command advances the cursor so the READY-row assertion cannot hold. Child receipt remains unmeasured until the probe lifecycle is corrected and tested. The active checklist remains in progress.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2: added a 3-point diagnostic that records initial gesture and direct retry outcomes separately. Tauri failed 3/3 direct retries and its later initial gestures; all three recoveries selected zero cells. Two Wails runs differed: one passed cycles 1 and 3, failed cycle 2 on both the initial gesture and direct retry, and selected zero cells on recovery; the next failed all three cycles, while one recovery selected all six cells and two selected none. Both successful and failed Wails recovery observations included the target native responder. Tauri also showed WebView ownership persisting for 20 seconds in a recovery attempt. The eight SGR sequences matched across first, direct-failure and recovery gestures within each host. These results show intermittent failure can occur before the current cycle's third-point click, can persist after focus settles, and can sometimes recover after a failure. The test records which phase failed and preserves the original failure when recovery diagnostics also fail. DTrace could not attach to the TUI child because System Integrity Protection is enabled and tracing requires additional privileges; child receipt and the TUI's internal selection state remain unmeasured. Cross-host Red remains open.
- V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.1 Red: added a tracked actual-TUI drag recorder with 60-cycle acceptance criteria, correlated DOM/PTY events, and measured final text-cell pixels. Rebuilt Tauri and Wails with TUI both select `header` cells 5–10 on the first drag, then leave zero selected cells on a direct drag after a third-point click despite matching eight reported SGR mouse sequences and complete DOM down/move/up. On both, moving to another card, returning, clicking the prompt, and retrying also leaves zero selected cells. Waiting for the third-point release did not resolve the Tauri failure; without the third click a later Tauri cycle also failed. Matching host results narrow the investigation to shared code, the PTY child, or the TUI. The checker compares pixels only in target text cells because the unrelated blinking cursor caused a false failure in the full-row comparison. Raw recordings are removed after measurements.

The remaining mixed frame was fixed under V5-96-5. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make native-test`, and `make docs-check` pass.

- V5-96-5: fix the Tauri mixed terminal frame during divider drags. Red reproduced native surfaces at the previous predicted x while the DOM card had advanced, even though `waitForPresentation:true` was recorded. The workbench layout queue now waits through the browser render tick and the matching host presentation before starting the next draw. The original 500pt drag passed all 3 capture sets (15 round trips) with unchanged pixel limits on the rebuilt Tauri host; each set recorded the generic image-composition barrier. `make -B tauriv2-build`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make native-test`, and `make docs-check` pass.

- V5-87-3: update the terminal settings window contract from 13 to 14 rows after adding the explicit notification policy.

- F8-6-2-1: start native surface preparation before the page draw and wait for its answer after DOM work is scheduled. Rebuilt Wails and Tauri pass 10/10 tracked divider repeats at the unchanged 34 ms limit.

- F8-6-2-1 progress: tracked repeats measured the remaining serialized presentation wait (Wails 34.1 ms at run 9/10; Tauri 109.4 ms at run 1 under high load). The 34 ms criterion remains unchanged and open.

- V5-87-2: correct native overlap activation evidence to avoid duplicate pointer delivery; `make -C native/darwin test-activation` passes.

- V5-87-1: add `terminal.notifications` with `tab` and `system` policies. Red was the disagreement between the terminal contract and old system-banner checks for executables outside an application bundle. Green validates the setting and passes it to `tab.notify`; tab reports expose system notices only to the notification center without a tab fallback, and invalid policies remain explicit errors. Workbench and terminal unit tests pass; rebuilt Wails and Tauri setting-transition checks pass for system success or native error, tab-only grid notices, and real tab-notice clicks. `make docs-check`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, and `make e2e-check` pass.

- V5-87: rebuilt Wails and Tauri real-input tiers pass 25/25 each, activation tiers pass 10/10 each, and `make -C native/darwin test-activation` passes. Fixes cover selection raster ordering, terminal scrollbar/history behavior, explicit notification policy delivery, Finder test window placement and reactivation, and native WebKit overlap measurement.

- V5-99: current rebuilt Wails and Tauri applications ran with dedicated disposable configurations and endpoint files. The complete `terminal.test.mjs` suite passed 37/37 on each host, including theme card-color and light/dark raster checks; terminal font, protocol, shell, and browser checks also passed, with no `Unknown operation` result. This closes the stale-sidecar retry condition.

- V5-94: current rebuilt Wails and Tauri applications ran with dedicated disposable configurations and endpoint files. The maximize/restore layout check passed on both hosts; the last behind-window frame was 47 ms/42 ms on Wails and 55 ms/43 ms on Tauri, within the existing 150 ms limit. The check compares current window, surface, and recorded-frame geometry without activating either check application.

- V5-96-4: fix the client watch/unwatch lifecycle. A Red test reproduced `watch()` resolving before the final `status.unwatch` acknowledgement; `Client.#release` now returns the unwatch promise and `watch.finish` awaits it, preserving both failures with `AggregateError`. `@soksak/client` passes 24/24, the focused browser theme check passes 2/2, and the subsequent full run passes the case on both hosts. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make e2e-check`, and `make docs-check` pass. The separately observed one-time Tauri divider gap is tracked as V5-96-5.

- V5-96-5 progress: the Tauri terminal divider check reported a 57px right gap at frame 8 once, passed 10/10 focused tracked repeats, and passed in the next full run. The later rebuilt reproduction identified and fixed the layout-queue ordering cause; the pixel criteria remain unchanged.


- V5-96-3: correct the footer pixel test to use capture `contentScale * scale` and limit the scan to the recorded content rectangle; the old canvas-width ratio selected a fixed window-bottom rule and reported 0pt movement on both hosts. Compare every visible settled surface frame with the initial frames in the cross-host drag test; its former first-surface-x-only condition accepted an intermediate layout with a different width. Focused footer checks pass 2/2 and the cross-host comparison passes 5/5 tracked repeats. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make e2e-check`, and `make docs-check` pass. The subsequent full 246-case run passes both corrected cases but finds three unrelated current failures, recorded under V5-96-4.

- V5-25-2: correct historical claims that AppKit button masks or HID-system modifier flags proved a person supplied input. Preserve the observed values and state explicitly that their source was not identified. Rename the real-input check to describe the posted HID state. Repository checks pass; approval-gated real-input checks were not run.

- F13-5-3: classify the completed F13 parent as an aggregate in the feature-evidence audit. The tracked regression proves that F13 is exempt and another completed top-level capability without a feature link still fails. Red reproduced the false error after F13 became `[o]`; the focused check and full `pnpm test` pass after the correction.

- V5-96-1: record each host's window frame and content size, scale, surface frames, document size, and layout-related effective settings when the cross-host settled layout comparison fails. The diagnostic makes unequal initial conditions observable without changing the assertion or its limit. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, and `make docs-check` pass; the window check awaits disposable endpoints.

- V5-96-2: make synthetic-input refusals report only observed state. Active-window drag refusals include the sampled pointer and frame; pointer-button error 1007 names AppKit's nonzero `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` mask without attributing its source. The exposure specification states that the source is unknown. `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, the focused geometry repeat, boundary, exposure, and documentation checks pass.

- V5-96 progress: the inactive-window native `capture_test` passes and records one complete frame. It does not reproduce the archived Tauri full-suite capture-stop timeout because it exercises a static AppKit window; the timeout remains unresolved pending a tracked run against the disposable Tauri endpoint.

- F0.5.6-1: fix the shared macOS window compositor to autoresize with its host when the host resizes the WebView directly. A Tauri-shaped Red test kept the wrapper at 800pt after its parent grew to 1200pt and hit a plain `NSView` at x=1100; Green follows the parent and hits the main `WKWebView`. The rebuilt pre-fix Tauri width sweep reproduced the toolbar failure from 1229pt. The tracked width test now keeps every sample inside the display; rebuilt current Tauri and Wails each pass all five native chrome clicks at 18 widths from 756pt through 1512pt, with inactive and non-key app states. Their maximum-width recordings contain 20 frames / 11.59ms longest gap and 24 frames / 22.83ms longest gap. Correct `make native-test` to run the declared VT sidecar artifact and report spawn errors immediately; its former stale `target/debug` path waited 60 seconds on a missing executable. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, and `make e2e-check` pass.

- Retired checklist entry `G1.3-10`: the entry recorded agent work-process rules instead of project work.

- G4.2-1: derive executable language coverage from tracked workspace files and make `make parity-check` reject missing or extra adapter languages. Add a shell adapter and module-local tests of the actual build-environment CLI; JS/TS 16, Rust 2, Go 15, Objective-C 5, and shell 2 tests pass. Record test ownership and mechanical language coverage in both agent guidance files and the verification specification.

- G1.3-9: teach the canonical checklist checker to accept `[!]` only when each language records a nonempty cause and retry condition. Tracked tests cover valid, missing, and empty fields; all checklist tests and `make docs-check` pass.

- V5-98: consume AppKit's `noop:` text command in the native terminal input view without committing pending composition or sending a sidecar command. Other unsupported selectors still return an explicit error. The native regression and full native test suites pass; documentation, boundary, and exposure checks pass.

- V5-97: a TUI-reported click clears a previous terminal selection while retaining the PTY mouse event. The native text input region removes its `NSTextView` cursor tracking areas, the terminal raster requests the I-beam, and pointer routing refreshes WebKit cursor tracking when its target changes or the application becomes active. A tracked real-input check samples the system cursor during 600 slow moves; two runs on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts each saw 2,400 I-beam samples and no arrows. Focused sidecar selection and mouse-report tests pass. The real selection check now starts inside the first cell before its midpoint; its old midpoint start copied `ELECTME` while incorrectly expecting `SELECTME`. On the final rebuilt hosts, the corrected real click, slow pointer, and TUI Shift-drag cases pass for both apps. The vt-core output contract check now consumes every presented image before expecting later PTY output; its previous fixture left sequence 2 in flight and timed out. The focused tracked repeat passes 10/10.

- V5-92, V5-93: native Escape closes the add and split picker through the modal document selector, and the project-directory check waits for terminal surface readiness. Focused tracked repeats and both corresponding full-window cases pass on rebuilt hosts.

- V5-95: the terminal raster places its cursor at the selected UTF-16 grapheme boundary of preedit; a marked `글은` with selection location 2 has its cursor after `은`. The native client commits a Korean suffix when its final character is not Hangul, including `은 `, and the sidecar sends AppKit `insertNewline:` as Enter instead of acknowledging it without input. Native frame and input tests pass; the activation suite passes 20/20 on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts, including the real `ddd한글은` cursor and Space commit.
- V5-88: expand the repository fallback audit across supported JS/TS extensions and product languages. Red/Green tests cover logical-OR classification, multiline value defaults, optional calls and nullish assignments, parameterized/multiline swallowed promise catches, inline Go blank assignments, and product frontend discovery. `--list` reports 306 occurrences with locations and stated reasons (218 `??`, 2 `??=`, 14 optional calls, 8 `||`, 60 Rust `unwrap_or*`, one Rust `.ok()`, two Go ignored secondary results, and one Objective-C catch); no occurrence is unresolved. Nine focused tests and the full workspace `pnpm test` pass, along with documentation, boundary, and exposure checks.
- V5-90: expect all 15 Go shell test cases in the language evidence manifest. The former count of 13 rejected a run where every case passed.
- V5-91: update seven divider gesture mutation targets after the explicit disarm invariant replaced optional calls; all seven mutations are caught again.
- V5-86-1, V5-89: the occlusion notification check covers each app with its own project window, the placement check asserts only the frames it sets, and a check that refuses a covered window names the covering windows.
- V5-84: a terminal drag selection covers a cell once the pointer passes the cell's midpoint, as in macOS Terminal; the sidecar maps selection points to the nearest cell edge.
- V5-85: `core.surface.document` reports `body` as the surface content size, larger than `viewport` when the content overflows.
- V5-86: both hosts notify `host.window` watchers when the window's occlusion changes; the spec states that windows at the same frame do not cover each other.
- V5-83-1 and V5-83-2: window checks place the Wails and Tauri check windows at the left and right of the screen so neither is completely covered, and a check requires the shell surface document to fill its native frame at rest and after a divider drag.
- V5-83: the full window suite passes 239 of 239 on Wails and Tauri rebuilt at bb27d49d. The window checks follow V5-75, V5-79, V5-81, V5-81-1, and F13-3-4: they find the shell surface by its input rule, terminals by an OSC 11 background, and selected cells by their background pixels; the pasteboard guard compares types regardless of order, and the inset sidebar check waits for the dragged layout before it clicks the divider.
- V5-83: the terminal raster fills the area outside its cell grid (right of the last column and below the last row) with the current default background, including a color a program set with OSC 11, instead of the theme background; `Screen.background` is required. Green: `make native-test` and the window check on rebuilt Wails and Tauri.
- V5-81-1: the terminal and the shell card draw in the card color and text color of the theme instead of the green stand-in color, and the themes no longer define separate surface colors.
- V5-82: the terminal protocol checks close their terminals, so their reply lines such as `R74:1b5b4f1b5b49` no longer stay on screen.

The browser empty state paints the card color instead of the window background.
- V5-79: a new terminal or shell in a project starts in the project's root instead of the home directory; a split still starts where the source surface was, and a window without a project keeps the home directory. Surface contexts carry `project.root`, and the shell sidecar's `open` accepts `directory`.
- V5-78: bookmarking from the file tree works from any tree of the project: the selection is the files state's `files.selection` (`files.select`), shown in every tree. Every tree drops a folder removed from `files.tree`, and `files.tree` no longer reads empty during a refresh.
- F13: complete the plugin sidebar sections and plugin-oriented settings window on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts. All nine browser, files, and shell sections expose their declared state; the manifest-based settings pages, sidebar set operations, compatibility window checks, and browser empty state pass on both hosts.
- F13-5-2: a click on the inset sidebar divider folds or unfolds at once; the double click and the host `pointerTiming` call are removed, and the folded divider shows a 2-point line.
- F13-2-6 (files): the file tree header uses card-header icon buttons (star, rotate-cw) through the new section context `icon(name)`; the tree has no extra padding, its header text and row chevrons line up with the section header's chevron, and rows use the sidebar's 12-point text and 20-point row height.
- F13-2-6 (core): a section row may declare `fill: true`; in `list` layout open fill sections share the sidebar height the other sections leave and scroll their body, and the file tree declares it.
- F13-1-1: each sidebar's selected tab and folded sections are saved with the space (`sidebars` in the space layout) and shown again after a reload or a restart. The `sidebar-sections.js` part landed in d5e71587.
- V5-76-1: the browser example opens a project without a page error. Without a host, surfaces that need sidecars or native regions show a placeholder instead of failing, the compositor receives the page's own placements, and `report` writes to the console. `make browser-example-check` opens a project in headless Chrome and fails on any console error.
- F13-3-5: the setting `railWidth` is removed; a new rail column starts at `sidebarWidth`. A settings file that still contains `railWidth` fails to load with "unknown setting railWidth"; remove that key from the file. The minimum, maximum, and starting width share the range 60–800 and read 최소 폭, 최대 폭, 처음 폭. Window checks restore the common settings they change.
- F13-5-2: the inset sidebar folds from its divider: a drag resizes, a single click folds or unfolds after the system double-click interval that the host now reports (`pointerTiming`), and a double click unfolds at `sidebarWidth` instead of the minimum. A folded sidebar keeps only its divider; the fold button, the inset status line, and the `sidebarFoldedWidth` setting are removed (the key was also removed from the check apps' settings.json). Tabs-layout tabs no longer wrap, and section names no longer move when a section folds. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts; the posted double click awaits the real-input tier.
- F13-2-5: 파일 트리 draws the project folder with `@pierre/trees` (bundled with its license texts): file-type icons, selection, a 4-point scrollbar, colors bound to the theme tokens, folders listed when opened, disk changes reconciled per folder, and git status decorations from the new `git` operation of the files sidecar (`files.git`). ☆ bookmarks the selected file. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts (`e2e/files.test.mjs`) and in dark and light captures.
- F13-2-4: the file tree follows the disk. The files sidecar watches the project folder and the expanded directories (kqueue on macOS) and reports changes, and the files state lists them again, so a file created or removed outside the application appears or leaves without 새로 고침.
- V5-77: an inset sidebar without a stored width opens at 190 points (`sidebarWidth`); the minimum stays 120, and a double click on the grip still sets it.
- V5-75: 사이드바 위치 (`rail`) defaults to `inset`, the sidebar inside the card, and its control lists 카드 안 first. A settings file that stores another value keeps it.
- V5-76: `environment.json` accepts `sidecars: false` for a runtime that cannot run sidecars, and validation rejects a plugin whose state module uses sidecars there. The browser example declares it and no longer lists the files plugin, so opening a project there no longer reports "requires exactly one declared sidecar".
- F13-2-3: the files plugin draws the project folder in 파일 트리 and its bookmarks in 북마크. A plugin can declare a `state` module, which the workbench mounts once for the shown project; it registers the plugin's statuses and commands in the application document, uses its sidecar with the project folder as `root`, and stores declared per-project `data` in `projects.json` under `plugins`. The new files sidecar lists directories inside the project folder. The tree updates after a folder toggle or `files.refresh`. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts (`e2e/files.test.mjs`).
- F13-5-1: sidebar section headers, the tab strip, and the status line use the card border rule so their outline is as crisp as the design; headers take the card header height and the status line the card footer height, so their rules line up with the neighbouring cards; the fold chevron is the core chevron-down stroke icon. A window check measures the rule contrast against the header background in a capture.
- F13-5: sidebars follow the sidebar design: no set title or place chip, 26-point section header bars that fold to the header, a 26-point status line at the end of every sidebar, empty lists in the muted UI font, and one rule between an inset sidebar and its card, with colors from theme tokens. New dom names `core.sidebar` and `core.sidebar.status`. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts with captures in dark and light modes.
- V5-74: a browser tab and the 탭 section show the document title, or the address when the page has no title.
- F13-2-2: the browser DOM section lists the document's elements and the 네트워크 section lists the requests its Performance API recorded (`browser.elements`, `browser.requests`), which a content-world script of the shared document view reports; `make exposure-check` accepts core names in section modules.
- F13-2-1: the browser 히스토리 section lists the session history of the document region and loads an entry on a click (`browser.history`, `browser.history.go`, document `go` action `entry`), and the 탭 section lists the browser tabs of the card and selects one on a click through `core.grid` and `core.tab.select`; sections may follow core statuses and bind core commands.
- F13-3-4: the settings window no longer repeats a control per plugin or section. 일반 holds 사이드바 위치, the left and right sidebar switches and sets, and the widths. 플러그인 is a searchable list of manifest names and descriptions (`description` is now required in `plugin.json`); a row opens the plugin page with its settings, sections, and left, right, and rail choices, and 목록 returns. A plugin's left or right choice (a set, 사용 안 함, or 일반 따름) takes precedence over the general choice while its card is focused. 사이드바 lists sets; the editor has the name, the layout, and section rows, each a select box grouped by plugin with ▲ ▼ −, plus +, through `core.settings.sets.row`, which rejects a repeated section. `core.settings.link` takes a set id, `off`, or `inherit`.
- V5-73: the browser home address is the plugin setting `홈 주소` (`browser.home`, empty by default) on the browser plugin page; a new browser card opens it, or starts with the empty state when it is empty. The manifest `home` key and the surface context `metadata` are removed, and plugin settings gain the `address` type.
- V5-70-2: document regions keep their site data in `document-data` inside the application's configuration directory instead of the application's WebKit container, so each `--config-dir`, including a disposable check directory, has its own cookies and caches.
- F13-2 (shell): sidebar sections draw real content. A section module receives `context.status(name, fn)`, which follows a status of its plugin on the card's tab (or the default surface) and reports a failed watch, read, or release as a page error, and `context.bind(element, name, params)`, which connects a control to its plugin's command under the dom name `core.sidebar.section.control`. `core.sidebars` reports each section's `text` and `controls`. The shell exposes `shell.history` (nonblank lines written through `shell.write`) and `shell.jobs` (pending `shell.run` commands); 실행 기록 lists the lines and writes one again on a click, cwd shows `shell.cwd`, and 작업 lists pending runs with a 중단 control. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts (`e2e/sidebar.test.mjs`). The browser and files sections still draw placeholders (F13-2-1 to F13-2-3).
- F13-3-1: stored sidebar `sets` and `links` pass the plugin-api `validateSidebars` and `checkSidebarReferences`, the same functions that check `environment.json`; a set naming an unregistered section or a link to a missing set or plugin fails the settings load with an error, and a change fails with -32602. Sidebars no longer skip unregistered sections.
- F13-3-2: a sidebar section's mount failure after release and its dispose failure are reported as page errors instead of being ignored.
- F13-3-3: every plugin setting declares a Korean `label` and, where needed, a `description`; the settings window shows them on the plugin page.
- V5-70-1: the Google appearance check no longer requires a light Google page. Measurement showed Google drawing its own stored dark page while the region received the light color scheme; document regions keep site data across runs. The check now requires the light color scheme in the region and an unchanged Google page across host themes, and reports the measured state when it fails.
- V5-72: the surface context offers the core icons as `icon(name)`, and the browser back, forward, and reload buttons draw the Lucide chevrons and rotate arrow at the size, color, stroke, and hover of the card header buttons instead of the characters ‹ › ↻.
- F13-3: the settings window has the sections 일반, 플러그인, and 사이드바. 합성 is removed together with the compositor commit delay and placement offset (`latency`, `skew`), `diagnostics.knob`, and `core.layout.reset`. A settings file that still contains `latency` or `skew` fails to load with "common settings: unknown setting latency" (or the same for a project file); remove those keys from the file. 플러그인 lists the environment plugins and shows each plugin's declared settings, its rail and right sidebar sets, and its sections; 일반 shows no plugin or sidebar setting. 사이드바 holds 사이드바 위치, the left and right sidebars, the widths, and the set list with create, edit (title, `list` or `tabs` layout, sections), and delete through `core.settings.sets.*`. The inset sidebar minimum, maximum, default, and folded widths and the new rail width are the settings `sidebarMinWidth`, `sidebarMaxWidth`, `sidebarWidth`, `sidebarFoldedWidth`, and `railWidth`.
- F13-1: every sidebar (left, right, rail, and inset) mounts the `module` of each section of its set with `{card, surface}`. A set's `layout` `list` stacks every section under a fold header (`core.sidebar.section.fold`); `tabs` mounts only the selected section (`core.sidebar.section.select`); fold and selection are kept per sidebar, and `core.sidebars` reports them. Section rows require `module`, sets require `layout`, and staging rejects a section module that the package does not publish. The nine sections draw a placeholder list until F13-2. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts (`e2e/sidebar.test.mjs` 4/4, `e2e/audit.test.mjs` 2/2).
- V5-71: the browser address field shows the loaded address after a navigation that did not come from typing, also while it keeps focus after Enter; text being typed stays until a navigation command runs or the field loses focus. The new status `browser.address.text` reports the field text and focus.
- V5-70: document regions send the Safari user agent of the same WebKit (`Version/<major>.<minor> Safari/605.1.15` from the system Safari, through `applicationNameForUserAgent`), so sites that select pages by browser name no longer send the reduced page; Google Search now receives its current page instead of the basic page with gray buttons. `document_view_test` checks the request header and `navigator.userAgent` (Red before: the bare WebKit user agent). Local `prefers-color-scheme` pages already followed the application mode in captured pixels on both hosts.
- F13-4: a browser card without an address hides its document region and shows the empty state `browser.empty` ("주소를 입력하세요") in its place; a click on it focuses the address field through `browser.address.select`, and the first load shows the region again. Before, the visible empty WKWebView took native focus on a click and drew white. Validated on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts (`e2e/browser.test.mjs` 14/14, `e2e/audit.test.mjs` 2/2).
- F13-0: a double click on the inset sidebar grip sets the minimum width 120 (the grip no longer prevents the default pointer action, which suppressed `dblclick`), a sidebar without a stored width opens at 120, and the real-input tool sends the click count so a posted double click is a real one.
- V5-69-2: the settings window and menus call card and sidebar places "위치", and the group descriptions say what each group changes.
- V5-69-1: the inset sidebar's fold button folds the sidebar, the grip lies over the border with no gap beside the surface, and dragging does not select sidebar text.
