The `operation: "cursor"` contract rejects invalid values and reports `blinkVisible` without recreating the PTY or changing cell metrics.
- Completed F5.2. A single terminal click now restores the target WebView before the next synthetic pointer after native image focus, and native image focus notifications plus modal focus restoration cannot synchronously re-enter the WebKit event path. Red reproduced terminal 2/3 pointer receipt timeouts and a modal-close deadlock; rebuilt Tauri and Wails focus matrices passed 4/4 bounded subtests each, covering three terminals, browser/tab switching, resize, settings modal close, project return, and a separate project window.
- Completed F5.1. Terminal surfaces now receive the effective application light/dark appearance through the declared theme subscription and `operation: "theme"` sidecar request. The sidecar changes default, cursor, and indexed ANSI raster colors without recreating the PTY, session, text, or cell metrics; invalid modes are rejected explicitly. Rust frame/engine/protocol tests, terminal module tests, and rebuilt Tauri/Wails pixel checks pass; the Wails surface theme binding is exposed through the host contract.
- Completed F4. Browser document facts now expose actual native first-responder focus, and direct `input.pointer` presses notify the owning surface for both Tauri and Wails instead of relying only on the native event monitor. Rebuilt Tauri and Wails focus-isolation checks pass 1/1 each; browser basic-operation checks pass navigation, address typing, and document scrolling on both hosts; the rebuilt native facts test reports document focus.
- Completed F0.5.7-1 and G2.4. Removed the surface presentation cycle from command settling, made hybrid controllers share one document paint-boundary token to prevent a MutationObserver loop, moved Tauri project/window work onto the AppKit event loop, and stopped Wails window preparation from reapplying stale webview dimensions. Rebuilt isolated project/window checks pass on Tauri (1/1, 3.12s) and Wails (1/1, 2.31s); normal shutdown passes in 65ms and 14ms.
- Corrected the F0.5.7 record: its command-settling surface-readiness wait created a presentation cycle (command settling → surface ready → native presentation → command settling) and was removed. F0.5.7-1 remains open for a non-cyclic contract and independent Tauri/Wails evidence.
- Completed G2.3. Surface binder audits now follow `parentNode` through ShadowRoots, so delegated shell controls are not falsely reported as commandless. The plugin-api suite passes 72/72, rebuilt Tauri and Wails audit checks pass 1/1 each, and normal shutdown passes in 66ms and 64ms. Other full-matrix failures remain explicitly open.
- Closed F1. The current rebuilt Tauri and Wails composition/reflow evidence is now recorded as one parent gate: divider and project-return captures pass without native border intrusion or shell white remnants, the Alacritty engine suite passes 27/27, and both host resize checks pass 1/1. Browser focus, terminal interaction, OSC/CSI, IME, restoration, and modal follow-ups remain separate.
- Completed F1.4. Added executable terminal reflow assertions for fixed renderer metrics, hard and soft newlines, Korean and wide cells, overflow/scrollback, and primary versus alternate screen isolation. The Alacritty engine suite passes 27/27, and current rebuilt Tauri and Wails window-resize checks pass 1/1 each with PTY, DOM plane, and native raster geometry verification.
- Completed F0. The startup and existing-basic-operation gate is closed for its stated scope: rebuilt Tauri and Wails child checks cover input, shell, browser, appearance, project/window lifecycle, ownership, protocol naming, and presentation, while broader Wails parity and composition/reflow remain separate.
- Completed F0.5. The completed child checks and the current rebuilt single-instance Tauri matrix now cover startup, prompt/output, controls, first-click native input, appearance and surface scope, first-run project opening, view timing, process ownership, and stale composition rejection. All bounded cases passed and normal shutdown removed the endpoint and lock.
- Completed F0.5.6. A bounded rebuilt Tauri matrix exposed a real SIGABRT: `project_open` and `window_new` were asynchronous commands, so AppKit window creation and activation ran inside a Tokio worker and a Rust foreign exception crossed the IPC task boundary. The commands and menu callbacks now perform AppKit work on the event-loop thread. Current Tauri terminal, shell, modal/control, browser navigation/theme, library/project, and normal-shutdown checks pass with per-case output; normal shutdown removes the endpoint and process lock.
- Completed G3. The host-pair audit is now injectable and rejects a missing host or counterpart in a fixture. Combined with the parity, language, evidence, and mutation injections, the tooling suite reports 80/80 passing tests with no skips or todos; package, documentation, boundary, and exposure gates pass.
- Completed G3-1. The audit self-suite now injects duplicate ownership, wrong behavior-test attribution, no-op and omitted-response implementations, mandatory skipped language results, and stale executable digests. The focused suite reports 36/36 assertions with structural inventory, language outcomes, mutation behavior, and content-hash evidence kept as separate results; the full package and repository gates pass.
- Completed F0.5.4 and F0.5.5. Tauri picker dismissal no longer deadlocks by recursively locking the modal state during discard. Tauri and Wails now enforce one application process per configuration directory with an explicit second-owner error, stale-PID replacement only after process absence, and normal lock cleanup. Bounded rebuilt Tauri modal, command, three-terminal input, same-directory refusal, and different-directory coexistence checks pass; the endpoint unit tests pass in both hosts.
- Completed G2-2. Added a named host-structure behavior test, linked G2-1 and G2-2 in the parity feature audit, and corrected the current G1.4 inventory record to 56 lanes, 249 implementation files, and 169 test files while preserving the earlier 56/250/169 result as historical evidence. The parity self-tests and full `pnpm test` pass.
- Completed F0.5.3. Removed the terminal surface's duplicate pointerdown focus binder; the card now owns the single focus gesture while `terminal.focus` remains an explicit command with observable error propagation. Terminal package and workbench tests pass, and the rebuilt Tauri three-terminal native keyboard check passes in three bounded runs without a second click. The previous full-run capture failure was an environment disk-full error and is not counted as a product pass.
- Closed G2-1. The Tauri persistent-service process probe now belongs to the platform endpoint contract and returns an explicit Windows unsupported error; the diagnostic-only Wails unit test is recorded as the H5 language-boundary exception because it tests an unexported helper without widening the host API. `make hosts-check` and the declared-environment Tauri host library test pass.
- Completed F0.6. Replaced the cross-language sidecar wire field `op` with `operation` across JavaScript/TypeScript, Rust, Go, Objective-C, Tauri, Wails, tests, fixtures, errors, and specifications. The legacy field is explicitly rejected without fallback. Full package, language, documentation, boundary, exposure, and native gates pass.
- Closed the G4 command-supervision item. The focused supervisor suite reports 16/16 passing assertions in 1.58s with no failures, skips, or todos, and retains explicit failures for nonzero exits, missing executables, timeout, cancellation, observer errors, output-drain failures, and denied cleanup verification. Language adapters and content-hash evidence remain open under G4.
- Closed G4.2 language adapters. `make language-test` now discovers and executes one explicit JS/TS, Rust, Go, and Objective-C case with 10s/30s/30s/30s limits, visible start/progress/final events, expected-versus-actual counts, and zero/skip/crash rejection. Current rebuilt output is JS/TS 16, Rust 2, Go 13, and Objective-C 5 tests, all passing with no skips or todos. The remaining G4 content-hash evidence item stays open.
- Reconciled the machine inventory after adding the language adapter implementation, manifest, and tests: 55 lanes, 249 implementation files, and 168 test files. The earlier 53/247/166 record remains historical under G1.3-2.
- Added schema-versioned language evidence records. `make language-test` now emits implementation, test, dependency, dirty-worktree, build-flag, process, expected/actual, elapsed, and attempt data; `--evidence-file` appends retries only when the immutable snapshot is unchanged and rejects stale records. The current inventory is 56 lanes, 250 implementation files, and 169 test files; the prior 55/249/168 result is historical under G1.3-7.
- Completed F0.4-1.3.3. The Rust failure audit now covers VT core, VT Alacritty, the Tauri host, and the Tauri application production lanes instead of leaving the remaining bridge paths implicit. The declared native build environment produced no ignored production `Result` outcomes, Windows unsupported operations return explicit errors, and the 23 parity self-tests and native workspace suite pass. Objective-C and cross-language failure-matrix work remain open under F0.4-1.4 and F0.4-1.5.
- Completed F0.4-1.3.2 for the Tauri host. The scoped Rust audit found ignored callback sends, transport reads, cleanup, capture-stop, and close-owner serialization outcomes. The production lane now returns or explicitly reports each outcome, recording abort preserves simultaneous stop and cleanup failures, and the Tauri Rust suite and parity audit pass. Remaining Rust host/bridge and Objective-C lanes stay open under F0.4-1.3.3 through F0.4-1.5.
- Completed the JS/TS failure-propagation audit for F0.4-1.2. The machine Red inventory found six empty or undefined promise rejection handlers in client unwatch, exposure release, layout queue, project switching, and transcript paths. Each path now propagates or reports the failure; the audit self-test, package tests, and repository gates pass. Rust and Objective-C lanes remain open under F0.4-1.3 and F0.4-1.4.
- Completed F0.4-1.3.1 for the VT sidecar. The Red clippy audit found ignored actor, session, monitor, and shutdown outcomes; the production lane now returns or explicitly reports each outcome, and the panic/shutdown contract test requires the failure to remain observable. The sidecar test suite and Rust failure audit pass; Tauri host Rust remains open under F0.4-1.3.2.
- Corrected Wails surface creation failure propagation: native creation errors and nil handles now return attributable errors, failed layout preparation is cancelled, newly created views are rolled back, and modal alignment no longer substitutes a zero rectangle after an alignment error. The remaining Rust and Objective-C audit lanes remain open under F0.4-1.3 through F0.4-1.5.
- Closed G1 after completing the workspace inventory, ownership enforcement, feature-evidence links, and their machine checks. Behavioral verification remains attributable to each linked feature and is not implied by the structural closure.
- Enforced independent implementation ownership for core, plugins, sidecars, native code, applications, and contract tests. The machine audit found and removed four plugin-to-sidecar implementation/test references, resolves a plugin's single declared sidecar in the host-scoped runtime, and rejects package-name or ambiguous-sidecar fallback. Ownership and focused runtime tests are Green; structural checks report zero ownership errors.
- Audit completed checklist claims against current evidence. Four stale or misleading records were found and preserved with explicit follow-up IDs G1.3-2 through G1.3-5: a parity test-file count, F3 scope wording, the Wails modal parity snapshot, and F0.1's dirty-worktree wording. The original claims remain unchanged until each correction has its own Red/Green evidence and commit.
- Reconciled the G1.4 parity record with the current machine output: 53 lanes, 247 implementation files, and 166 test files. The parity checker now fails when the recorded counts drift, and its 15 self-tests cover both the matching and mismatch cases.
- Corrected F3.1/F3.2 scope wording to preserve the historical sequence without reporting the later-closed F3 checks as currently open. The parity audit rejects both stale present-tense phrases and its 16 self-tests cover clean and injected-stale records.
- Qualified the 2026-09-21 Wails modal parity Red as a historical snapshot and stated the current F10.2 correction without deleting the original failure. The parity audit rejects an undated snapshot or one that omits the current correction; 17 self-tests pass.
- Replaced F0.1's stale dirty-worktree wording with the committed build, preserving the original scoped observation. The parity audit rejects the stale phrase or missing commit identity; 18 self-tests pass.
- Complete the feature-evidence audit for every completed capability. The parity checker now rejects a completed checklist capability without an implementation entry point, named behavior test, expected result, and verification level; the aggregate review record is explicitly excluded. The 41-link audit, 15 audit self-tests, workspace tests, boundary, exposure, and documentation checks pass.
- Browser document webviews now receive explicit host dark/light appearance updates in both native hosts. The F3 application pixel and restore/new-document checks remain open.
- Fix native document visibility after restoring a surface: the host is made visible before document geometry is reapplied. The current Tauri browser E2E previously observed the correct frame with `visible:false`; the native regression and rebuilt browser navigation/input/scroll/placement/isolation check now pass. F3 controlled-site pixel and restore/new-document checks remain open.
- Apply the current host theme when creating a native document and persist each browser surface's explicit HTTP(S) location. Rebuilt Tauri and Wails browser E2E pass controlled-site light/dark pixel checks for existing, new, and reloaded documents; invalid stored locations fail explicitly.
- Verify Google Search site-preference isolation: the disposable profile's explicit light preference stays at `[255,255,255]` while the host changes light→dark, and the Google URL is unchanged on both rebuilt hosts.
- Close the terminal resource/recovery gate with an explicit failure-domain boundary: application loss/update reconnects to the surviving shared service, while a service crash is reported as service failure and never replaced by an unclaimed new session.
- Replace fixed sleeps in the normal-shutdown window check with endpoint filesystem events and status notifications. The check now has rejecting case timers, reports elapsed time, and requires both application PID and endpoint disappearance. Current rebuilt Wails and Tauri checks pass in 14ms and 25ms; the full script suite is Green.
- Strengthen the three-terminal divider check to five uninterrupted narrow-to-wide-and-back round trips in each of three project-return sets, require observed range movement and a card beside the divider to reach the default 96pt minimum card edge plus 12pt gap, and link the check as feature `F1.3`. Preserve each prepared DOM/native layout snapshot and process draw transactions in arrival order so fast input is not silently replaced. Current rebuilt Wails and Tauri checks pass all three `ms:96` sets with 11 endpoint transitions per set and direct composition assertions; the previous Wails 7/11 Red is fixed.
- Keep native surfaces hidden during layout preparation and reveal each surface only in the matching presentation ticket after the application DOM has presented. Tauri and Wails host contracts carry the declared visibility through preparation and presentation; host compile and focused tests pass, while rebuilt-application capture evidence remains pending.
- Reject window evidence when the endpoint process started before the current executable build. The focused stale-process check now fails explicitly instead of measuring an older binary as current evidence; three contract assertions and the stale Tauri window attempt confirm the rejection.
- Link the bounded declared normal-shutdown check to its implementation, named behavior, expected result, and application verification level in the parity audit. The audit now inspects 25 feature links; the repository-wide link set remains explicitly incomplete.
- Add `e2e/normal-shutdown.mjs`, a bounded check for the declared `command.run` → `host.quit` path. It waits for one ready window, requires a `null` reply, and verifies that the rebuilt Wails and Tauri application PID and endpoint disappear within the five-second case limit. An undeclared direct `host.quit` endpoint call is not treated as lifecycle evidence.
- Verify repeated project/library returns on current rebuilt macOS hosts. The Tauri case passes in 0.98s and Wails in 2.99s while retaining one app DOM, two browser documents, one terminal service PID, three shell PIDs, and all terminal session IDs across three returns. This does not claim normal service shutdown; that remains a separate gate.
- Return the Wails diagnostic capture cap state as `limited` alongside the actual frame count and longest gap. Reaching the cap remains a normal bounded result; Wails payload tests cover capped and uncapped responses, and the rebuilt diagnostics host passes its focused tests.
- Complete asynchronous shell command validation on both macOS hosts. Serialize injected pointer requests, drain pending WebKit mouse work before registering the current receipt, wait for the release-generated click, and keep explicit unreceived errors. The focused shell e2e passes all three cases on Wails and Tauri; native, workspace, boundary, exposure, and documentation checks pass.
- Restore first-focus address selection and first-release selection retention in the mounted browser module, preserving caret editing on later clicks and disposing handlers. Bind the selection command through the registry. The module replaces the old page entry. Two new regressions failed before correction; nine browser package checks and rebuilt macOS Tauri navigation, native replacement input, scroll, placement, and isolation pass. Terminal, shell, settings, and add/split menu basics also pass on this dirty implementation; Wails integration remains pending.
- Keep native terminal focus through pointer release by cancelling the DOM anchor's default focus action, without stopping card-selection propagation. Declare and bind `terminal.focus`, and expose failures in session status. Two unit regressions reproduced Red; rebuilt macOS Tauri passes three-terminal native editing and consecutive commands without a second click. Wails focus integration and cursor rendering are not validated by this result.
- Remove the obsolete surface-document offset from exposed DOM rectangles. Mounted plugin modules share application coordinates; independent document origins remain intact. Two coordinate regressions failed before correction; all 16 registry tests and package tests pass, and both hosts build. Rebuilt Tauri confirms the corrected coordinates and first-terminal native typing; remaining terminal focus and browser address-selection failures are recorded separately, not reported as an application pass.
- Add bounded command-group supervision with start/progress/final events, elapsed time, raw output forwarding, timeout/cancellation, and cleanup evidence. Reject permission-denied probes as proof of process absence. Sixteen supervisor assertions pass, including the Red case for incorrect EPERM success. Per-language case adapters and full process ownership checks remain pending.
- Discover source and test languages outside fixed audit roots and expose unmapped files as structural failures, without claiming behavior parity. Add regression cases for six omitted-file categories and duplicate ownership. Run actual Rust terminal package tests instead of an empty successful command, and include audit self-tests in the root test command. The 22 inventory/checklist/package-command assertions and package suite pass; the repository inventory and Tauri native-keyboard acceptance remain red.
- Enforce one canonical checklist and synchronized translated task states in docs-check. Reject duplicate identifiers, invalid states, reordered/nested differences, and reopening or deleting completed identifiers; follow-up issues retain their completed predecessor. All nine checklist assertions pass after eight invalid transitions reproduced failure.
- Register Wails `CompositionDeclare` in both the main-page binding and surface bridge, validate and store its immutable contract before placement, and keep the runtime method map in sync. The rebuilt Wails app no longer fails surface mounting with `unknown host call: compositionDeclare`; its three-terminal project-return and rapid-divider checks pass alongside the rebuilt Tauri checks. Native composition tests, documentation checks, and diff validation pass.
- Reap terminal PTY children independently of the PTY output reader. A child that exits while its PTY master remains open is now waited by a dedicated reaper, and normal close joins both the reader and reaper before removing the session. The new rebuilt-host check passes on Tauri and Wails: three terminal PTY children are created, closing all terminal tabs leaves the shared service PID unchanged, and no PTY child or zombie remains.
- Include logical native containers in window diagnostics instead of enumerating only WebViews. Read geometry, container identity and image facts in one UI-thread snapshot so attachment closure cannot invalidate handles between measurement steps. The three-container assertion failed before correction and passed afterward. Clear all plugin attachment state on app DOM reload without closing persistent terminal sessions; application reload and reconnection validation remains pending.
- Correct observed single-DOM startup and surface-creation failures: use the public named AppKit appearance API, keep the surface-port registry in its consumers' scope, create and then canonicalize the configuration directory, register the Wails main view after the native window exists, release Tauri's lookup mutex before inserting a surface, and resolve Wails window handles to their registered app WebView. A new wrong-handle native test reproduced the crash before correction; all 29 surface-host assertions passed afterward. Application observation still reports plugin-mount integration failures; these component results do not establish working terminals.
- Replace URL-origin-based native presentation ownership with the window's single app DOM. A native regression test reproduced a 3.007s delay from a separate same-origin document; after removing that wait, it completed in 0.009s while retaining the app DOM and open-transaction waits. Eleven component assertions passed. Rebuilt-host composition validation remains pending.
- Reject malformed terminal input encoding with the base64 library's strict decoder instead of silently accepting a valid prefix. The new malformed-input test failed before correction; both encoding tests passed afterward.
- Forward native image events to their owning Tauri surface instead of discarding them in an empty callback. Preserve modified-key characters in immediate and queued terminal input. Expose `terminal.screen` notifications and replace repeated screen requests in window checks with a current read followed by status notifications; preserve blank-cell widths when reconstructing test text. The new native-input check failed before correction and passed afterward on both macOS hosts with three terminals each (8 cases, no failures or skips), covering typing, Backspace, Ctrl+U, Enter, output, and isolation. Package tests, native tests, both builds, and structural checks passed. Composition verification remains separate.
- Preserve an existing surface's native frame and snapshot while hidden instead of applying an invisible 1×1 viewport. Defer image configurations while its outer surface is hidden, and refresh visible image rasters from actual native geometry before presentation. Native state tests cover hidden configuration and refresh eligibility; a three-terminal window check compares frames and snapshots before and after entering the library.
- Keep outer-surface visibility when clearing image regions for the first document load or navigation. Both host unit tests reproduced removal of the hidden state before the first attachment, which incorrectly configured a hidden 1×1 image. The window harness now requires fixture presentation before reloading and collects presentation-error logs, so reload cannot clear a failed preparation and hide it from the test result.
- Restore the application-document presentation wait before native layout commit, keep image pixels at their original scale, retain native presentation errors in region facts, and use fractional visual-viewport dimensions for region insets. Commit and cancellation run on the native main queue because a Tauri event callback can deadlock when the commit synchronously redraws its window. A sampled library-check failure established that reentrant lock path; the document wait itself was not the deadlock.
- Keep failed layout requests rejected and visible in verification/error state while allowing the next explicit user operation. Do not reuse rejected preparation promises or present the same ticket twice. Package tests cover failure recovery, request ordering, fractional viewport insets, and preparation before DOM drawing.
- Strengthen native-capture checks: three terminal regions in each frame, zero border intrusion or white pixels, stable glyph dimensions and pixel count, and every requested round trip. Repeat five-trip drags and project return three times; reject presentation errors even if a later operation clears them. Synthetic measurement fixtures reject a stationary screen, missing terminal, one-pixel border intrusion, white stripe, and stretched or missing text. The existing page-driven diagnostic gesture remains unchanged and is not evidence of OS button state. Current measured results and unresolved validation failures are recorded in [feature status](docs/features.md); no full release pass is claimed.
- Define and implement one surface-composition boundary for DOM-only and hybrid plugin pages. Every surface now has one clipping `SurfaceHost`, a native plane for declared document and image regions, and one DOM plane above it; declared DOM overlays are the only DOM input owners above native documents. Plugin manifests declare the complete composition, the page API submits complete revisioned snapshots, and both hosts reject partial, stale, or undeclared operations. Image raster dimensions come only from applied native geometry. The hosts retain immutable snapshots during continuous resize, reject stale frames again on the main thread, and make `host.window.presented` wait for the current visible raster. The terminal no longer computes or sends image sizes. Native tests cover fractional 2×/1× coordinates, last-device-pixel input, clipping, overlay hit ownership, raster changes, and immutable image presentation. Package, boundary, exposure, platform, host, native, activation, and full library checks pass; both macOS host builds pass. Rebuilt-application window checks are pending.
- Add image regions: native surfaces can display images supplied by sidecars through IOSurface tokens. The page calls `attachImageRegion(element, name, sidecar)` to attach a region under a surface; the sidecar sends image envelopes through the host with token, dimensions, and a sequence number; the host validates the image, releases the previous one after presenting the next, and sends errors for invalid tokens, dimensions, or access denial. The sidecar receives keyboard, composition, and focus events from the region. Both applications add a terminal plugin that renders to an image region using a PTY daemon (`sidecars/ptyd`) shared by both hosts and the shell sidecar. Add `packages/plugin-api/image-region.js` and `page.attachImageRegion`; PTY daemon manages session lifecycle (detach keeps sessions alive, close ends them) with idle timeout (60s debug / 5min release / `PTYD_IDLE_TIMEOUT` override); terminal sidecar (`@soksak/sidecar-vt-core`) implements a terminal emulator with `open`, `input`, `resize`, `screen.read`, and `close` requests. Window checks include `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` for terminal input through the shell sidecar. `e2e/browser.test.mjs` updated to pass with the new image region and terminal plugin. Package tests, `make docs-check boundaries hosts-check native-test`, and the Wails macOS build passed; the Wails application draws the terminal at the window's device scale. Keyboard input through the region does not work yet; the Tauri application and the terminal window checks are not verified.
- Show web documents in document regions instead of URL surfaces ([native surfaces](docs/spec/native-surfaces.md#document-regions)). `plugin.json` `surface` accepts only `page`, and `home` names the first address of a surface page; `surface.url`, the workbench `external` flag, and Tauri external surface URLs are removed. `native/darwin` adds `document_view.m`: a web view inside the surface web view with its own persistent data store, http and https navigation only, state reports with the scroll position from an isolated content world, placement by viewport insets, and dialog blur. Both hosts add `DocumentAttach`/`document_attach`, place, load, go, and detach calls, check that the caller is the named surface, send `document-state` only to that surface, close regions when a surface is removed or its page commits a new document, and report regions in `host.window` `documents` and `host.hit` `{kind: "document"}`. The Tauri document commands run off the main thread, and cleanup started on the main thread does not wait. The plugin API adds `attachDocument` (placement from `ResizeObserver`, resize, and scroll events), `ownManifest`, and `page.document` in both runtimes. The browser plugin is now a page with an address bar, `browser.location`, and the `browser.navigate`, `back`, `forward`, `reload`, and `stop` commands; its toolbar uses the card tokens. The inventory lists `_setOverrideDeviceScaleFactor:` on document views. `e2e/browser.test.mjs` checks navigation, history, address typing, native scroll without activation, placement after surface moves and resizes, isolation between browser surfaces, and closing with the surface; the settings check now expects the browser point to belong to the document region. Package tests, host tests, `make native-test`, `make release-check`, `cargo check` for macOS and Windows with and without `diagnostics`, and Windows `go vet` passed. Document regions take their frame from their insets whenever the surface changes size; autoresizing had lost the insets after the surface was smaller than them. A region reports `loading` from the request of a navigation until it starts or fails, because a replaced load reported a finished load of the new address before it started.
- Measure and report more of what the window checks depend on. Capture frame files store the rectangle the window occupies in the buffer, the content and backing scales, and the display time. `diagnostics.capture.start {display?}` records around any request, and with `display` records this application's windows on the window's display, which keeps recording in a full-screen Space. The capture reports blank and suspended frames, copies frames in its handler and writes them on a separate queue (slow 2× writes of up to 480ms had dropped frames and caused the intermittent frame-count failures), and `diagnostics.capture.stop` returns `longestGap`; a drag recording with a gap over 100ms fails. `sp_input_activate` reports the step that stopped (refused, not key, webviews pending, lost) with the frontmost application, and both hosts put it in the 1006 message. `host.window` adds `maximized`, `host.screens` adds `visible`, and `host.window.fullscreen` enters and leaves full screen. `e2e/resize.test.mjs` records maximizing and restoring and reports frames whose layout is behind the window frame (250–400ms in both hosts); holding the window's Core Animation transaction until the layout presented kept frames matched but slowed the AppKit animation, could not pace the Wails display-link animation, and stalled pages that keep drawing, so it was removed.
- Make native input arrive in order and at the requested distance. A press or release from `input.pointer` answers after a script in a separate WebKit content world reports that the document received it: with a text field focused, WebKit passes mouse events to the input method asynchronously, and a press and release sent together reached the shell page in the opposite order. A scroll is delivered after the target view has presented, because WebKit does not scroll a new document with an earlier wheel event. Wheel distances for web views in the device-pixel surface container are converted to the container's units in the application's event monitor and in native scroll input; at 2× every surface and document region scrolled half the distance. The page runtime sends sidecar messages in call order.
- Fix hangs and races. The Tauri host held the modal view lock while waiting for the main thread in `host.window`, while the main thread waited for that lock to place the modal. The endpoint `command.run` waits for the main page without its own 10-second limit, because the main page answers forwarded commands within their declared timeout; requests to a main page that is not ready or reloads end with 1003. Core commands answer after the draws they scheduled, so `core.boundary.move` no longer returns before the cards move. `soksak-stage` replaces staged sidecar executables with new files; overwriting a file that had run made macOS kill the new process. The browser check waits for the page title and for the full scroll distance.
- Open saved spaces that name plugins or sections the environment does not register ([projects](docs/spec/projects.md#persistence)). The workbench drops their tabs, rails, and rail widths, closes a card left without tabs or gives it a new tab, skips their sections in set names, and leaves them out of library previews. Before, such a space or preview failed with `unknown plugin` or `unknown section`, for example with data saved before the terminal plugin became `shell`. `e2e/projects.test.mjs` checks both hosts.
- End both hosts cleanly on termination signals ([endpoint](docs/spec/endpoint.md#discovery)). SIGTERM, SIGINT, and SIGHUP ended the process with the default action, leaving `endpoint.json` and the socket; Wails v3.0.0-beta.16 creates its signal handler but never starts it. `OnTermination`/`on_termination` in `platform/darwin/termination.*` now turns the first signal into the quit that `host.quit` runs, which saves ready windows first, and lets later signals end the process. The Wails default handler is disabled, and the Tauri host adds `signal-hook`. Before listening, both hosts remove sockets of ended processes of the same application. `tests/termination_test.*` checks the signals in a child process and `tests/endpoint_test.*` the removal. Three existing clippy warnings in the Tauri host are fixed, and the host tree in [native hosts](docs/spec/hosts.md) now lists the document region and termination files.
- Wait for committed layouts before reporting a presented window, and record up to the presented screen ([native surfaces](docs/spec/native-surfaces.md), [exposure](docs/spec/exposure.md)). A newer surface preparation extends the window's open layer transaction, so a presentation update could finish while the screen still showed the previous layout; the maximize check failed in 3 of 12 and 2 of 20 runs on Tauri with a last frame of the previous size. `native/darwin` adds `surfaceLayoutAfterSettled`, which waits until the window has no open or queued preparation, then for the presentation update, then reads the next refresh time from the screen's `CADisplayLink`; both hosts use it for `host.window.presented`, which now returns `{displayed}`. `diagnostics.capture.stop` takes `after` and stops once the stream has delivered the screen at that time, and the harness passes it for drags and resizes. `resize.test.mjs` removes each recording right after measuring it and restores the window in a session cleanup, because the after hooks of a test run after the session closes its connection; a failed restore had skipped the removal and left 21 GB of recordings, which filled the disk and made later recordings stall. The maximize check then passed 30 consecutive runs.
- Apply modal content and positions in the order the host changed them ([native modals](docs/spec/native-modals.md)). A modal document receives its first content as a call answer and later changes as events; an answer that arrived after a later position event moved settings back to its opening position, which failed a Wails command check once. Both hosts number each content and position change, and `workbench/modal-order.js` drops older values. `core.modal` `document` adds `loaded`, and debug builds add `diagnostics.modal.hold` and `diagnostics.modal.held`, with which `modal.test.mjs` delivers the first answer after a move; it failed on both hosts before the change. The Tauri `overlay_content` command no longer runs on the main thread.
- Shorten the window zoom animation in both hosts ([native hosts](docs/spec/native-host.md)). The window frame and the web content are presented separately, so the whole animation showed the previous layout: `resize.test.mjs` measured the layout behind the window for 263ms in Wails and 432ms in Tauri. AppKit reads the length from the `NSWindowResizeTime` user default, in AppKit's own zoom and in the Wails window's replacement animation; 0 is ignored, so `native/darwin/src/window_motion.m` registers 0.001 before the first window and a value the user set still wins. The layout now follows within 116ms at worst, and `resize.test.mjs` fails over 150ms; 20 consecutive runs passed. `native/darwin/tests/window_motion_test.m` compares the resize time of a process that registers the value with one that does not (0.350s and 0.003s).
- Keep a surface at the frame the host applied ([native surfaces](docs/spec/native-surfaces.md)). Attaching the Web Inspector to a surface web view made WebKit move that view to the rest of the window and leave it there after the inspector closed: in a running application the shell surface became the full window width (0,250 1512×228 instead of 412,121 428×357), and `core.verify` reported `V7b applied == declared` off by 1083px. `webviewSetFrame` now remembers the frame of views in the surface coordinate container and restores it when something else changes it, so an open inspector overlaps the surface instead of moving it. Modals keep following the window, because a dialog modal resizes with it. `native/darwin/tests/webview_inspector_test.m` opens and attaches the inspector through `_WKInspector` and checks the surface frame; without the change it measured 0,250 1000×100.
- Let AppKit own the window buttons ([native hosts](docs/spec/hosts.md#window-buttons)). The hosts moved the buttons into a view of their own to centre them in the page's 45pt first row; AppKit took them back whenever the window title or the recording indicator changed, and the frame in between reached the screen: a recording of window moves and resizes showed the buttons missing or at the title bar position in about one of 48 changes. Each window now gets an empty toolbar with the unified compact style, so the title bar is 40pt and AppKit centres the buttons in it; `windowUnifiedTitlebar` replaces `windowPlaceControls`, and the container that held the buttons is gone. The page takes the first row height from the button area (`--chrome-h`), so the row follows the title bar. `native/darwin/tests/window_controls_test.m` checks the centring after a resize and a title change, and `e2e/controls.test.mjs` checks it through six rounds of moving, resizing, maximizing, and renaming in both hosts.
- Keep the first row and full screen in step ([native hosts](docs/spec/hosts.md#window-buttons), [exposure](docs/spec/exposure.md)). Taking the first row height from the button position made the row 1884pt in full screen, where AppKit moves the buttons out of the row: the hosts now answer the page with the button area and the title bar height, and the page keeps its row height when there is no title bar. `host.window.fullscreen` answers after the transition; macOS ignores a request made during one, so `sp_window_fullscreen` applies it when that transition ends. The name starts with `sp_` because Wails compiles its own `windowFullscreen` and the linker had picked that one, which made the host report a failure while the window did change. `native/darwin/tests/window_fullscreen_test.m` and `e2e/controls.test.mjs` check both.
- Save a project window's position in window points in the Tauri host ([projects](docs/spec/projects.md#persistence)). `windowState` returned the size in points and the position in physical pixels, so on a 2× display the saved numbers named a place twice as far from the screen origin; a window at x 755 was stored as 1510 and reopened against the right edge of the 1512pt screen, where the layout had 57pt content columns. The Wails host already stored points, so the same file meant two places. `e2e/projects.test.mjs` now compares the saved geometry with the window frame the host reports, which failed on Tauri and passed on Wails before the change.
- Draw the rail outline in the plane's CSS pixels ([native surfaces](docs/spec/native-surfaces.md)). The outline is an SVG path in an element that covers the plane, and that element carried a coordinate system the size of the grid. When the window grows, the element follows the plane before the page draws again, and the previous path is fitted to the new box: a recording of a full-screen transition showed the outline around the enlarged layout while the cards, sidebars, and surfaces were still at the previous size. Without that coordinate system the numbers in the path are CSS pixels, so the outline stays with the cards until it is drawn again. `packages/workbench/test/rail.test.mjs` checks that neither the page nor the plane gives the outline a coordinate system that scales with its element.
- Package tests, `make docs-check boundaries platforms hosts-check e2e-check exposure-check native-test`, `make release-check`, `cargo check` of the Tauri application for macOS and of the Tauri host for Windows with and without `diagnostics`, Windows `go vet`, and `make -C native/darwin test-activation` pass. `make -C native/darwin test-activation` needs a yielding application in front; it reported the Tauri check application, which activates at launch, as frontmost and passed after Terminal was brought back. On rebuilt macOS debug hosts with the built-in 2× display, two consecutive window runs passed all 60 checks with none skipped, and no recordings remained.
- Serve status, commands, and DOM elements through a local endpoint in both macOS hosts ([exposure](docs/spec/exposure.md), [endpoint](docs/spec/endpoint.md)). The endpoint is a Unix socket announced in `<config-dir>/endpoint.json` and speaks length-prefixed JSON-RPC 2.0; no method runs code. Add `packages/client`, the `soksak` CLI, and the stdio MCP server `soksak-mcp`. Diagnostic methods exist only in debug builds (Go tag and cargo feature `diagnostics`). The TCP control port, the `--observe` family of flags, and the probe `eval`, `evalAsync`, and `mouse` operations are removed. A connection's subscription changes are applied in arrival order in the client and both hosts; before this, ending and restarting a watch could leave the page unsubscribed. The page sends transcript lines in answer order, which made the host transcript comparison fail intermittently. Opening the library now finishes before a project switch loads the plane; before this, a project opened meanwhile lost its plane and its window failed to close.
- Add native input to `native/darwin` and both hosts (`input.pointer`, `input.key`). Keys use `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]`; presses, drags, and releases go to the view under the point; scroll is a windowed event made with the undocumented CGEvent window-number field and `CGEventSetWindowLocation` and sent through the window. WebKit updates hover only in the key window, so a move without a button to another window returns 1006; `activate: true` activates the application and waits for WebKit's active state (`_doAfterActivityStateUpdate:`) first. Private declarations are collected in `native/darwin/src/private/` and listed in the [private native API inventory](docs/operations/private-native-apis.md). Surface and modal pages no longer take the keyboard focus when they focus an element (`_setShouldSuppressFirstResponderChanges:`), Tauri creates those webviews without focus, and `host.window` reports a modal as shown only after it has the focus; a terminal surface that finished loading had taken Escape from an open menu. `make -C native/darwin test` runs the checks that do not activate the application; `make -C native/darwin test-activation` activates it for the hover and overlapping-webview checks, which no longer fake the key window.
- Centre the window buttons on the first row from their visible size (y 22.5 in content coordinates) and place them when the page is ready. The previous y 14.5 assumed 16pt buttons and left the 14pt buttons 1pt high, and the first window kept the system position until its first resize. AppKit takes the buttons back when the window title changes; they are now returned before the next display instead of after the next layout, which let the system position reach the screen for up to 80 ms. The pending return finds the window again, which fixes a crash after a window closed.
- Window checks run only through the endpoint (`make e2e-check` rejects code execution, ports, polling, and fixed sleeps) and always delete recordings. Captures are recorded in sRGB, stop only after a frame displayed after the stop request, and keep only completely written frames; the Tauri drag waits for the settled commit as the Wails drag does. The alignment colours now come from the theme instead of pixel values of a previous display. The skipped external busy-script checks are removed; `tests/surface_layout_test.m` checks that the presentation wait excludes external and hidden documents. Go and cargo now relink when the native library changes; before this, Wails debug builds could link stale native code. On rebuilt macOS debug hosts with one external 1× display, two consecutive window runs had 36 checks: 32 passed, 2 failed (fractional footer checks that require a 2× display), and 2 skipped (display-scale transitions that require two displays).
- Move the native host code from `apps/wailsv3` and `apps/tauriv2` into the library packages `packages/host/wailsv3` (Go module `github.com/min-median-max/soksak/packages/host/wailsv3`, package `host` in `src/`) and `packages/host/tauriv2` (crate `soksak-host-tauriv2`, `[lib] path = "src/host.rs"`). Both hosts keep code in `src/`, OS code in `src/platform/{darwin,windows}/` behind a platform interface, and tests in `tests/`; Go OS packages register from `init`, and Rust selects OS modules with `cfg` and `#[path]`. The applications contain only `src/main.*`, `environment.json`, `runtime/`, tests, manifests, and Tauri configuration; `src-tauri/` is removed, and both applications stage the frontend into `src/frontend/`. The Wails binding service name is now `github.com/min-median-max/soksak/packages/host/wailsv3/src.Host`. Add the root `go.work` and the root Cargo workspace with one `Cargo.lock`, the shared `[patch.crates-io]`, and output in `target/`; both application executables and the sidecar executables are placed in `target/debug` or `target/release`. On Windows both hosts return `is not implemented on windows` errors except for directory identity, and application startup fails; the Tauri Windows rounded-corner code is removed. The Tauri `gen/schemas` no longer list the dialog plugin because the application crate no longer depends on it. The shell sidecar selects its shell through `src/platform/{darwin,linux,windows}/`, and `packages/client` selects its transport address module through `platform/platform.js`. Add `make platforms` (`scripts/check-platforms.mjs`), which reports OS conditions and OS file suffixes outside `platform/<os>/`, and `make hosts-check` (`scripts/check-hosts.mjs`), which compares the two hosts and the two applications against the allowed differences in the [native host specification](docs/spec/hosts.md). Package tests, `make boundaries`, `make platforms`, `make hosts-check`, `make native-test`, and both macOS builds passed. `go vet` and `go build` of the Wails host for Windows passed, and `cargo check` of the Tauri host for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` passed without warnings; the Tauri application crate for Windows stops at the Windows resource compiler (`llvm-rc`), which this macOS machine does not have. With only an external 1× display connected, the window checks ran 38 tests: 22 passed, 14 failed, 2 skipped; the failures are the same set as the previous 1× run, and the terminal, library, project-window, and host-comparison checks passed.
- Move the shared macOS sources to `native/darwin/src` without the `_darwin` suffix and merge the two window-capture implementations into `native/darwin/src/capture.m`; both hosts call it through the library. The library minimum is now macOS 14.0 because capture uses ScreenCaptureKit APIs from 14.0. The shell sidecar keeps its code in `src/` (package `src/shell` and the `src/main.go` entry point) and its tests in `tests/`; the standalone input check is `tests/webview_input_test.m`. Both native builds passed, the shared input check passed, and both hosts recorded about 100 frames per drag through the shared capture; the terminal window checks passed in both hosts.
- Move sidecar declarations from `environment.json` to plugins: `plugin.json` lists sidecar package names, each plugin declares them as `package.json` dependencies, and each sidecar package provides `sidecar.json` with `executable` and `protocol`. `validateEnvironment` no longer accepts `sidecars`. Both hosts resolve sidecar executables from the staged `environment.json`, `plugin.json`, and `sidecar.json` files and identify sidecars by package name, such as `@soksak/sidecar-shell`. `soksak-stage` copies each `sidecar.json` and, with `--executables`, each built sidecar executable. Add `plugin.json` `preview.ink`, which replaces plugin-specific CSS for library preview colors in the workbench. Runtime modules export `windows`, which removes the workbench's native and browser branches and the `browser:` project identity. The browser runtime identifies folders as `path:<path>`, so projects saved by the browser application before this change fail the directory check and must be added again. Add `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, which checks that core packages do not name plugins or sidecars and that plugins and sidecars name only their declared dependencies. Rename the layout library test card fixtures to `upper` and `lower`. Package tests, `make boundaries`, `make native-test`, and both native builds passed. On rebuilt macOS hosts with only an external 1× display connected, the terminal, library, project-window, and host-comparison window checks passed in both hosts; the full window run had 22 passed, 14 failed, and 2 skipped, where the 4 failures beyond the earlier 2× baseline are the footer checks that require a 2× display and two Tauri recording measurements.
- Add the native probe operation `evalAsync`, which returns the value of a page promise through `callAsyncJavaScript`, and the `terminal.test.mjs` window check for terminal input through the shell sidecar. Show failed sidecar requests in the terminal output. Resolve window-check binaries from the harness location; the previous paths skipped every host check. The library check imported the removed runtime path and accepted the resulting TypeError as a rejected project creation; it now imports `@soksak/runtime` and requires two host rejections, and passed in both hosts. Against rebuilt macOS hosts the window checks ran 38 tests: 26 passed, 10 failed, 2 display-transition checks skipped. Both terminal checks passed, and removing the Wails sidecar executable made the Wails terminal check fail. The 10 failures also occur at 22c313b, the commit before the workspace split: the page verifier reports the 45px title row with native buttons at y 14.5 (14.5 above and 16.5 below), which fails the button-centre checks and the shake checks, and the Wails alignment and busy-script checks cannot measure the terminal line. Failed checks retain full recordings; this run's retained recordings filled the disk and truncated later frames until they were removed.
- Move shell sessions from the two hosts into the `sidecars/shell` process. Both hosts relay line-delimited JSON between surface pages and declared sidecars, deliver sidecar messages only to the window that owns the surface, report removed surfaces, and stop sidecars on exit. Pages use `page.sidecar(name)`; the terminal plugin declares the `shell` sidecar and native applications declare it in `environment.json`. Sidecar protocol tests (Go 3), Wails relay tests with a fake sidecar (Go 3 new, 6 total), Tauri relay tests (Rust 3 new, 6 total), and package tests passed; both native builds include `soksak-shell`. Native window checks and terminal input in the running applications were not run.
- Set every workspace package and application version to 0.0.1. The Tauri crate and configuration were set to 0.0.1 in the workspace split commit.
- Restructure the repository as a pnpm workspace. `packages/soksak` holds the layout library and its specification; `packages/workbench` holds the workbench frontend; `packages/plugin-api` defines `plugin.json`, `environment.json`, the staged layout, and the page import map; `plugins/{browser,terminal,files}` declare the three plugins; `apps/{browser,wails,tauri}` assemble them through `environment.json`; `native/darwin` builds the shared macOS code as a static library found through pkg-config; `e2e` holds the window checks. Install the library with `pnpm add "github:min-median-max/soksak#path:packages/soksak"`; the repository root is no longer a library package.
- Declare macOS 14.0 as the minimum for the native applications. The Wails capture code uses ScreenCaptureKit APIs from macOS 14.0 and the shared library uses a WebKit API from macOS 12.0, but the previous builds linked with an 11.0 minimum. The shared library build fails when a source uses an API newer than its declared minimum. Both native builds link with a 14.0 minimum and no deployment-version warnings; Wails still reports a duplicate `-lobjc` library from its dependencies.
- Remove plugin names and default layout from the workbench. Applications declare plugins, the initial space, and sidebar defaults in `environment.json`; each application supplies its runtime module instead of runtime detection. Remove the Wails source symbolic links. Package tests (plugin API 7, workbench 8, plugins 7, applications 8, library 310), the shared input check, both native builds, and the browser application (`verify: 17 pass`) passed. Native window checks were not run for this change.
- Move Global/Project scope selection from the settings navigation to horizontal tabs at the top of General. Preserve scope across categories, keep project opening mode in Global, and use Global labels and shared text sizing for override reset controls. Library screens now apply and edit common settings only, including after leaving a workspace; returning restores project overrides and both scope tabs. Rebuilt macOS hosts passed all 18 related settings, project, library, and modal checks. Native library settings were visually reviewed. `make docs-check` passed.
- Validate simplified library screens in both rebuilt macOS hosts. Creation, saved-project selection, window reuse, search, pins, and schematic preview checks passed. The full example run passed 37 checks, failed 2 for insufficient recording frames, and skipped 2 display-transition checks. The failed checks passed on rerun with unchanged code and criteria; intermittent frame-count failure remains unresolved. Go 3/3, Rust 3/3, and documentation checks passed.
- Unify library and workspace typography, letter spacing, line height, spacing, borders, corners, buttons, fields, and icon styling through shared CSS. Remove the library-only type scale and fixed theme shapes. Both screens scale their text with the configured base size. Rebuilt macOS hosts passed 39 example checks with 0 failures and 2 display-transition skips; visual comparison and font/size/corner changes were checked in both hosts. `make docs-check` passed.
- Display the project library at startup and in new windows. Create or select a saved project in the same unassigned OS window. Return from the workspace to the project list without closing its shells.
- Render schematic library previews from the saved card grid with uniform gaps, equal row tracks, compact sidebar columns, and theme-derived colors and content symbols. Remove stored renderer rectangles and rail paths. Retain project search, sorting, and persisted pins. Remove the library navigation sidebar, category filters, standalone folder-opening action, and Git clone implementation.
- Add Dock New Window, the library footer action, and Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+N. Remove the top New Window icon. On macOS, closing all windows keeps the application available for Dock New Window.
- Record native window checks at backing-pixel resolution. Point-sized downsampling blended thin separator colors and caused Wails alignment measurements to fail. Keep the existing pixel tolerances and gesture requirements. This changes diagnostic capture, not application layout.
- Persist common settings in the application configuration directory and explicit project overrides in `.soksak/settings.json`. Removing an override restores inheritance. Project opening mode remains common-only. Native hosts use JSON files; the browser example uses IndexedDB.
- Identify projects by canonical directory and filesystem identity. Reuse the existing project and window for duplicate opens, including concurrent requests. Save project order, spaces, cards, tabs, sidebar and rail widths, and normal window geometry. Startup displays the saved projects in the library.
- Open projects in independent OS windows through public framework APIs. Scope surfaces, settings menus, input, themes, and shells to each window. Complete pending saves before closing a ready window or quitting the application. Settings and add/split menus remain native webviews inside their project window.
- Serialize per-window native presentation on the UI thread. Complete pending presentation before removing a project's surfaces. Apply common or project settings only when their values change. Prepare the Wails main view before restoring saved window dimensions.
- Rebuild and restart both macOS hosts; the 41-check example suite passed 39 checks with 0 failures and 2 display-transition skips because only one 2× display was connected. Go 3/3 and Rust 3/3 storage tests, Wails Windows cross-compilation, and `make docs-check` passed. Verify application quit, final saves, saved library display after process restart, and Dock New Window after closing all windows. Windows and Linux native execution remain unverified. No private API or framework fork was added, and the layout library is unchanged.
- Add the private native API inventory with a necessity review, exact callers, framework dependencies, and update verification steps. Require it as the first document reviewed after native update failures. Correct the input-monitor comment about mouse-exit delivery. Runtime behavior is unchanged; `make docs-check` passed.
- Window checks wait for the initial terminal document, its theme, and native presentation before recording.
- Render macOS content webviews in device-pixel native coordinates while preserving CSS dimensions and device-pixel ratio. Native surface and document sizes now match at half-point heights. Remove native backing-background recoloring.
- Redraw the DOM layout when display resolution changes, including when its CSS size remains unchanged. Remove the resolution listener when the view is destroyed.
- Verify fractional footer pixels throughout a complete divider drag, document geometry after resizing and display-scale transitions, and native pointer coordinates in the final device pixel. Both macOS hosts passed these checks in `make examples-verify` (35/35).
- Exclude document-local adopted stylesheets when copying modal CSS. Settings navigation previously copied the main document's blur into the dialog. Both macOS hosts passed 12/12 modal checks with the new navigation assertions.
- Render settings and add/split pickers in webviews inside the main window. Settings uses a full-viewport 50% black CSS backdrop and 3px blur in each background document; menus retain transparent backgrounds without blur.
- Preserve modal ordering after surface replacement, move the settings card within its webview, resize it with the parent, and remove the modal and blur on main-document reload. Settings closes only with ×.
- Apply complete native surface rectangles and card DOM in one layer transaction after the main and visible application documents confirm presentation. External documents retain independent content rendering. Remove intersection shrinking and transparent rendering from ordinary surfaces. Hide surfaces whose next slot cannot be measured before changing the DOM.
- Ignore outdated DOM draw callbacks when a later layout preparation exists. Return the compositor's preparation promise to its caller.
- Maintain native button centering when AppKit reparents buttons during recording. Position queries no longer change layout.
- Record through the final visible return position of a driven gesture. Require complete round trips, zero measured overflow, and consistent terminal foreground, card, sidebar, and rail geometry. Verify repeated main-layout changes during the external document's 700ms task.
- Preserve background state delivered before the background script initializes.
- Remove the native visual-effect backdrop and the delayed-cursor diagnostic. The retained overlapping-webview input check tests pointer and keyboard routing.
- Subscribe to terminal output before starting the shell. This is a separate terminal startup correction.
- Separate specifications, operating procedures, feature status, and this change record. Add paired documentation and `make docs-check` to the required checks.

The main-only presentation check allowed a Tauri view to commit at 562pt while its document clipping layer remained 558pt. Shared presentation now includes visible application documents. After this correction, `make examples-verify` passed 29/29 on macOS 26.6.2, including background initialization, modal behavior, window controls, alignment, and main-layout progress during external work. Four additional alignment runs passed 16/16. The earlier Wails background initialization failure is fixed. Manual settings blur was confirmed in both hosts on 2026-09-07. Windows and Linux execution is unverified. These changes have not been released.

Library validation on 2026-09-08: `make verify` passed, including 310/310 tests, all 142 mutations detected, documentation checks, build, and comparison of generated artifacts with committed files.

## 2026-09-22

- Tauri now completes native surface-document and image cleanup synchronously when the main document starts reloading. This prevents replacement surface image attachment from racing teardown of the previous native image handles; repeated split and raster recovery checks pass on a rebuilt process.
- Workbench verification now distinguishes an unseated DOM layout from a measured geometry failure. It reports the explicit native-placement pending state while waiting, then evaluates V7 and rail bounds after the host answer.
- Unsupported OSC selectors now produce explicit sidecar rejection events, including fragmented BEL/ST input; the pinned selector inventory and named engine tests record the distinction from implemented and vendor contracts.
- Rebuilt Tauri and Wails runtime audits now cover every visible command-bound control and plugin document state; both pass without unbound controls. Wails still reports two observable post-test writes after its endpoint closes during cleanup.
- F10 is now closed as a parent gate: the rebuilt Tauri and Wails modal lifecycle, transparent picker, semi-transparent settings scrim, focus, ordering, reload, movement, resize, and geometry-verifier evidence are complete under F10.1–F10.4.
- Completed F6.3-7. Persistent terminal surface close now removes its registry entry instead of leaving a dead actor available for reconnect. PTY reader/reaper cleanup no longer occupies the Tokio blocking pool. Rebuilt Tauri sequential terminal checks report START/PASS for all three cases in 1.85s (3/3 passed).
- Completed F7 BEL/ST response-order evidence. The Alacritty engine test covers BEL and ST termination, indexed-color effect/query, CSI cursor-position query, and ordered replies without adding a second parser.
- Completed F7.5. The CSI inventory now links `J/K` to an executable erase-display/erase-line case. The focused Alacritty test passes 1/1 and asserts that each erase changes only the requested visible cells; the remaining CSI categories stay explicitly open.
- Completed F7.6. The CSI inventory now links `@/P` and `L/M` to an executable character/line insertion and deletion case. The focused Alacritty test passes 1/1 and asserts neighboring cells remain correct; the remaining CSI categories stay explicitly open.
- Completed F7.7. The CSI inventory now links `I/Z` to an executable forward/backward tabulation case. The focused Alacritty test passes 1/1 and asserts movement between tab stops; the remaining CSI categories stay explicitly open.
- Completed F7.8. X11/Tektronix OSC 13–19 now have a named explicit-rejection case. The macOS sidecar records them as unsupported platform operations rather than silently claiming an effect.
- Completed F11. The current exposure, runtime binder, bounded-capture, language-lane, boundary, documentation, and parity audits pass; F11-1 and F11-2 provide the behavior evidence without claiming unsupported host behavior.
- Completed F7.9. The CSI inventory now links `6n/c` to the existing ordered cursor-position and device-attribute response case; the full Alacritty engine suite passes 54/54.
- Completed F7.10. The CSI inventory now links `b` to an executable repeat-character case; the focused Alacritty test passes 1/1.
- Completed V1. The canonical checklist and its Korean translation are mechanically synchronized, completed scope cannot be reopened, and the current documentation audit passes.
- Completed V2. Paired-host structure, rebuilt Tauri/Wails first-raster presentation, exposure/parity, and language-lane evidence now have a named feature link; restoration, IME, and final repeat/release scopes remain open.
- Completed F7.11. The CSI inventory now links private application-cursor, mouse-report, and bracketed-paste modes to an executable set/reset state case; the focused Alacritty test passes 1/1.
- Completed F7.12. The terminal inventory now records VT application keypad controls as `ESC =`/`ESC >`; the focused test verifies set/reset state and prevents an unsupported CSI `?66h/l` assumption from being treated as success.
- Completed F6.3-8. A Tauri native image `notFound` now requests a fresh raster configuration, and an already-detached frame is returned as explicit `stale` invalidation. Rebuilt single-process Tauri new/four/repeated/failure-recovery split checks pass 4/4 without presentation errors; endpoint disconnect diagnostics remain explicitly identified as cleanup lifecycle output.
- Started F6.3-9. A new report of a Tauri split remaining in `loading` with `1005: the window did not present within 10s` conflicts with the prior single-process green evidence. The follow-up records the required one-binary/one-config reproduction, exact presentation state, and elapsed START/PASS/FAIL output before any new correction is accepted.
- Completed F6.3-9. A single current Tauri binary and one configuration directory passed the newly split, four-terminal, and repeated-endpoint cases three times (9/9), with explicit START/PASS timing and no `1005`, `notFound`, `notAttached`, or native presentation error. The reported freeze did not reproduce on the matching build, so no speculative code change was made; forced endpoint teardown diagnostics remain separate.
- Started V4. The existing project lifecycle case covers many persistence behaviors but does not explicitly prove two independent initial states with create/use/close/recreate in the same application instance. A separate bounded case is now required for both hosts.
- Completed V4. The new two-state lifecycle case passes on rebuilt Tauri (1/1, 1.75s) and Wails (1/1, 1.45s): each creates, uses, closes, removes, and recreates two independent temporary projects in one application instance under a 30s test limit.
- V5 Red evidence: `pnpm test` passed in 1.2s and `make native-test` passed in 167s. `pnpm breaks` exceeded its 180s lane because the runner repeats the full library suite for 142 injected breaks; it is not recorded as pass.
